    - Fit of the garter snake and water hyacinth cards (issue #103): on a trip, both cards fit every supported canvas, as every area fact card does; the owner shortened the garter snake title to "Meet garter snakes" so it stays on one line and the card fits 320-tall landscape screens (C13). The water hyacinth body, like the heron's, wraps at the tight-padding width at 320 x 440 (above), and the garter snake body wraps at the body width. In the Collection Book, the title hides on the found water hyacinth card's page (above); the garter snake card's page keeps it on every supported canvas. With Plum and the two cards, the Collection Book has 33 pages at 1 row per page and 21 at 5 rows per page.
15. **C15** First-find cards explain what each kind of treasure is for. There is one card each for the first Decoration, the first Wearable, and the first Paint shell, and each shows once per save (open question 94).
    - A kind's card is due when the player has banked at least one treasure of that kind and its card has not been seen. This is checked in the Home Tank right after the tank arrival card's OK, before the hub takes input (open question 93). So a save that already owns one sees the card once, after its next trip. Cards never show mid-trip, or when the Home Tank opens from the title screen.
    - Due cards show one at a time, in this order: Decoration, Wearable, Paint shell (open question 95).
    - Each card has a bold title, a short body, and the 32 pixel icon of the first banked treasure of its kind beside the title, with its rarity glow (B2; open question 98). The wording (open question 96):
        - Decoration: "You found a Decoration!", with the Decorations section line (C11) as its body.
        - Wearable: "You found a Wearable!", with the Wearables section line as its body.
        - Paint shell: "You found a Paint shell!", with the body "It unlocks a Dream colour: a magic paint for your axolotl. Try it in Restyle." On smaller canvases the body shortens to "It unlocks a Dream colour. Try it in Restyle.", and on the smallest the title shortens to "New Paint shell!".
    - The Decoration and Wearable cards have one "OK" button. The Paint shell card has "Try it now!" (primary, focused first) and "Maybe later", which closes the card (open question 97). "Try it now!" opens restyle on the Dream colour tab (F6, F14) for the explorer, or for the first owned axolotl when there is no explorer. Done or Cancel there go back to the Home Tank with that axolotl focused.
    - Every card button ignores presses for the first 0.8 seconds the card shows, so the press that closed the tank arrival card cannot press it. Tab, Shift+Tab, Left, and Right move the focus. Enter and Space press the focused button, ignoring key repeat. Escape closes the card. Touch works too. The cards appear and leave at once, with no motion, and fit 460 x 320 and 320 x 440.
    - A card is saved as seen as soon as it closes, by either button (D1). "Start over" erases the save, so the cards show again in the new game. With device storage off, the seen cards last only for the page session.

### D. Save and load

1. **D1** Progress saves on the device by itself after banking, going home, being carried home, beating an area, finishing the character creator, any tank change, any outfit change, any rename or restyle, any Dream colour change, choosing the explorer, choosing an area on the area map (N2), any Collection Book unlock, and closing a first-find card (C15). There is no save button. Treasures banked at the Home Spot mid-trip are saved at once. A rescue banked mid-trip is saved when the trip ends (open question 57).
2. **D2** Saved: banked treasures (including owned Dream colours), the starter and rescued axolotls, each axolotl's name and look (real morph, applied Dream colour, boy or girl, face, gill style, spots, tail, body, age, and size), each axolotl's outfit, the chosen explorer, decoration placements, Collection Book unlocks, which first-find cards were seen (C15), the stars earned for each area, the last area chosen on the area map (Area 1 by default; open question 112), the fact cards found, which are the hazards and predators met and the refuge fact cards found, in one list (C14; open question 119), and settings (none yet; reduced motion follows the device). Not saved: the Trip Bag, the position mid-trip, leg state, Shell blocks used this trip, the refuge's pending clean-ups and milestones (N2), and each area's trip memory: its trip seed, its eaten snacks, its stranded axolotl left waiting, and its lost treasures. Each area keeps that memory by area id, for this play session only (open questions 56 and 117). Which areas are unlocked, and how far the refuge is cleaned up, are not saved either: both come from the saved stars (N2). The look is plain data, with each option held by its stable id.
3. **D3** Closing or reloading and then reopening restores saved progress exactly. The game opens in the Home Tank, never mid-trip. Unbanked finds from an unfinished trip go back into their area and can be picked again (B4). They are not sure to be on the next trip after a reload.
4. **D4** Saves hold no personal data, never leave the device, and need no account or network, per [ADR 0007](../adr/0007-kid-safety-and-privacy.md).
5. **D5** If there is no save, the game starts fresh. An unreadable save never crashes the game, and the player can still play. The game starts fresh with no message. A damaged save is kept on the device until the next "Start over". A save from a newer version is left untouched and is never overwritten (open question 54).
6. **D6** Saves from an earlier version still load after an update.
7. **D7** "Start over" erases saved progress only after a two-step confirmation. The wording works for kids and for parents. It is a button on the title screen, shown only once an axolotl is owned (open questions 51 and 52).
8. **D8** A note that parents can read says progress is kept only in this browser on this device, and that clearing browser data erases it. It is on the first "Start over" card and in the README (open question 53).

### E. Updates

1. **E1** After a new version is deployed, a player who reloads or reopens the game gets it within one reload. Or they see a friendly "A new version is ready" prompt that loads it in one tap.
2. **E2** An update is never forced mid-trip. The prompt only appears on the title screen or in the Home Tank.
3. **E3** Updating never loses saved progress.
4. **E4** The version number is shown in small text on the title screen or in settings.
5. **E5** Any update check contacts only the game's own website, sends no player data, and fails silently when offline.

### F. Character creator and outfits

1. **F1** On first launch, and after "Start over", the character creator appears before the Home Tank. It goes step by step, one choice per screen, in 11 steps in this order: Colour, Boy or girl, Eyes, Mouth, Gills, Spots, Tail, Body, Age, Size, and Name. Each step screen has:
    - A heading "Step N of 11: ..." (for example "Step 3 of 11: Pick your eyes!").
    - Progress dots that show done, current, and to-come steps by shape and fill, not by colour alone.
    - A short helper line.
    - Every option of the step, all visible at once, as big picture buttons (up to 8 per step). Each picture button shows a small preview of that choice on the axolotl so far. The chosen one is marked by a thick ring and a tick. There are no arrows to flip through options. On the Boy or girl step, each picture button also shows a boy or girl sign as well as the axolotl and its label (F16), so Boy and Girl are never told apart by colour alone.
    - Back and Next buttons. The last step has Done instead of Next.
    - A "Surprise me" button.

    "Surprise me" on the first step, Colour, picks a whole new axolotl at random: Colour, Boy or girl, every look option, and a suggested name that fits the boy or girl (F17). It is never exactly the current axolotl. On every other step, "Surprise me" changes only that step's choice:
    - On a look step (Eyes to Size), it picks a different option of that step, each other option equally likely.
    - On Boy or girl, it always switches to the other one. If the name is a suggested name that does not fit the new boy or girl, it becomes a random suggested name that fits. A suggested name that fits, or a typed name, stays (F17; open question 109).
    - On Name, it picks a different suggested name that fits the boy or girl, ignoring capital letters. A typed name can be replaced by a suggested one.

    "Surprise me" always gives a different choice, because every step has at least two options. Nothing else changes: no other look option, colour, Dream colour, or outfit, and the step and the Outfit view stay. The focus is on "Surprise me" afterwards, because pressing any button focuses it. The helper line ends with one sentence that says what "Surprise me" picks on that step, for example "Surprise me picks new eyes." (open question 110). The player can then accept it or keep changing options. The creator starts with a random boy or girl, a random suggested name that fits it, the default look (open question 22), and the Leucistic colour. The creator ends in the Home Tank with the new starter axolotl. It still opens from one place (`openCreator` in `src/scenes/sceneFlow.ts`).

    Text and touch targets never shrink to fit (X5): targets stay at least 48 by 48 CSS pixels, and primary actions at least 64 pixels tall. When a step does not fit, it makes room in this order:
    1. The helper line hides.
    2. The picture button labels hide, and one caption names the focused or chosen option.
    3. The preview shrinks to 0.6 of its size.
    4. The heading drops its "Step N of 11:" start.
    5. The preview hides, and the chosen picture button is the preview (F3).
    6. (restyle only) The heading hides; the bold current tab names the step (F6).

    Every step fits the smallest canvases, 460 x 320 and 320 x 440.
2. **F2** The character creator offers:
    - Name: free text typed in the name box, or a pick from a list of suggested names (F17).
    - Boy or girl: exactly two choices, Boy or Girl. It is always set, and there is no way to skip it. A boy has a clearly taller, longer tail fin. A girl has a clearly rounder body and belly, and bigger eyelashes. The eyelashes are white-backed, so they read on dark morphs. A girl also always has a small pink heart on her near cheek, below and behind the eye. The heart is part of her look, never a Wearable, and takes no slot. Its ink outline is white-backed, so it reads by its shape in greyscale. A boy has no extra cue (issue #87). The boy and girl cues read at every size, on all eight real morphs, in colour and in greyscale. This replaces issue #52's rule that the cues are kept subtle (issue #58). The heart shows on every Girl picture, the Boy or girl step's picture buttons included. Collection Book portraits and silhouettes are boys, so they do not show it ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) 6u; open question 128).
    - Eyes: happy, sleepy, determined, curious, or grumpy. Grumpy has a lid that slopes down toward the snout, with only the lower half of the eye showing, and a short, gentle brow angled down toward the snout. It looks sulky and cute, never angry. Its brow and its half-shown eye tell it apart from determined (issue #87; open question 129).
    - Mouth: smile, open "D", tongue-out, small "o", big grin, cat smile, smirk, or blep. A big grin is a wide, filled grin, wider than open "D". A cat smile is a "w". A smirk is a one-sided smile. A blep is a closed smile with a small tongue tip.
    - Gill style: fluffy, long, or short.
    - Spots: none, freckles, dots, or patches. Freckles are a cluster of many tiny specks on the shoulders. Dots are a few even, medium, round dots along the back and tail. Patches are two or three big, soft blotches over the back and tail. Spots stay off the head, and never look like morph marks (the Wild flecks, the Copper speckles, or the Piebald patches).
    - Tail: Short, Medium, or Long tail length.
    - Body: Slim, Regular, or Chubby. Each has a clearly different outline at every size and age.
    - Age: Baby, Young, or Grown-up. Age changes proportions only, never size. A Baby has a bigger-looking head, from a shorter body and tail, shorter gills, and stubby legs. A Grown-up has longer gills. The head and face anchors never move.
    - Size: Small, Medium, or Large (F4).
    - Colour: one of the eight real morphs: Leucistic, Wild, Golden albino, White albino, Copper, Piebald, Axanthic or Melanoid.

    Every combination of options reads in greyscale and keeps the eyes, the gill roots, and the Wearable anchors and keep-clear zones ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) 6i) clear. Text about one axolotl says "he" or "she" to match its boy or girl, never "they" or "it".
3. **F3** A preview of the axolotl updates straight away whenever an option changes. Under reduced motion, the preview is a still pose. When a step has no room for the preview (F1), the chosen picture button is the preview. The options on a step can be told apart at a glance, in colour and in greyscale. The Spots step's picture buttons show the body and tail up close.
4. **F4** Size is for looks only. Large is the old Medium (scale 1.00), Medium is the old Small (0.85), and Small is new (0.72). The default size is Medium. Small, Medium, and Large axolotls have exactly the same hit area and the same speed, on trips and everywhere else. The tilapia hit area matches Medium for every size (see Tuning values). Boy or girl, tail, body, and age are for looks only too. The leg-loss puff, the regrowth twinkles, and the snack speed-up star sit on the leg root at every size, age, and body. The protected ring and the carry bubble scale with the size and keep their clearances. These are visual only (issue #49).
5. **F5** The starter's morph decides which morphs can be rescued: the other seven real morphs become the stranded axolotls (C2). The starter's morph cannot be rescued again.
6. **F6** In the Home Tank, the player can rename any owned axolotl and restyle its face (eyes and mouth), gill style, spots, tail, body, age, and size, by touch and by keyboard. Restyling uses the same step screens, picture buttons, options, and live preview as the character creator (F1 to F3), with these changes:
    - A row of tabs, one per step, replaces the progress dots and the Back and Next buttons. Each tab is at least 48 pixels tall. When the tabs do not all fit, 48 by 48 pixel arrow buttons page through them. The current tab is marked by a bar and bold text, not by colour alone.
    - Choosing an axolotl in the Home Tank, by a press or by Enter or Space on the focused axolotl, opens its axolotl choices card (issue #82; owner decisions 2026-10-20 and 2026-10-21). The Home Tank dims under ink at 0.45, and the dim takes every press. A centred white panel is titled with the axolotl's name. Its buttons come in this order: "Restyle" (pencil icon), which opens restyling on the Eyes step; "Outfit" (shirt icon), which opens the Outfit tab on its slots view; "Dream colour" (paint drop icon), shown only when at least one Dream colour is owned, which opens the Dream colour tab; and "Back" (left chevron). The buttons are square tiles (open question 134). Focus starts on Restyle. The arrow keys, Tab, and Shift+Tab move the focus, wrapping. Enter or Space presses the focused button, ignoring key repeat. Escape is Back. Every button ignores presses for the first 0.8 seconds the card shows, so the press that opened it cannot choose; Escape works at once (open question 135). The card appears and leaves at once, with no motion. Text and buttons never shrink, and the card fits 460 x 320 and 320 x 440 with its title. Back and Escape return to the Home Tank with that axolotl focused.
    - Restyling starts on the Eyes step. From the Paint shell first-find card's "Try it now!" (C15), it starts on the Dream colour tab.
    - The Colour and Boy or girl tabs open read-only screens. The colour and the boy or girl are fixed after creation and never change.
    - A restyle-only "Dream colour" tab comes right after Colour (F14). Its picture buttons are "Real colour" first, then each owned Dream colour in catalogue order. Each picture button shows that colour on the whole axolotl, its swatch, and its name label. The helper line says Dream colours are not found in nature. With no Dream colour owned, it shows only "Real colour", and the helper line says "Find Paint shells in the canal to get Dream colours!". It shows at most 8 picture buttons per page, with a "More colours" button that shows the next page, like "More names" on the Name step; "Real colour" is first on page 1. Its labels never hide (fit step 2 does not apply). When it still does not fit after the other fit steps, it shows fewer picture buttons per page. Done keeps the Dream colour picked. Cancel leaves without keeping it.
    - A restyle-only "Outfit" tab comes right after the Dream colour tab (C10, F8; open question 68). It opens on its slots view: one picture button for each of the eight slots, in F8 order, each with the slot name as its label. A worn slot shows the Wearable on the axolotl and its perk icon. An empty slot shows a dashed outline of the slot and a plus badge. Under them, the axolotl's outfit summary (F10), or, when that does not fit, one caption for the focused slot ("Head: Sun hat, Speed +4%" or "Head: empty"). Picking a slot opens its choices view: "None" first, then every banked Wearable for that slot in catalogue order. Each choice shows its name and its perk icon and text, and "On <name>" when another axolotl wears it. Picking a choice puts it on at once, and the view stays open. "Back to slots" returns to the slots view. A slot with no banked Wearable shows only "None" and the helper "No <slot> items yet. Find some on a trip!". It shows at most 8 picture buttons per page, with a "More slots" or "More wearables" button for the next page. With Area 5's Wearables (B3), a slot has up to 7 choices (Head and Back: None and six Wearables; issue #103). Its labels, perk icons, and perk text never hide (fit step 2 does not apply). When the choices view still does not fit after the other fit steps, more steps follow: 7. the tab row hides, and "Back to slots" moves above the picture buttons; 8. each label sits beside its picture instead of under it; 9. only when nothing before it fits, one choice a page, with "More wearables" beside the picture and the label block under them, wrapped at the band width (owner decision on issue #103, comment 6374010919). A label block under its picture wraps at 168 pixels. A label block beside its picture wraps at 168 pixels where that fits, else wider, up to the band width less 60 pixels (204 pixels at 320 x 440; open question 152). At 460 x 320 and 320 x 440 the choices view shows 1 choice a page, with "More wearables". At 320 x 440, with 2 to 7 choices, it takes fit step 9, the only canvas that does; with 1 choice ("None" alone) it keeps step 8. Every layout that fit before step 9 was added is unchanged. Done keeps the outfit, and takes each Wearable in it off any other axolotl that wore it. Cancel leaves without keeping it.
    - Every restyle step shows the axolotl in the outfit being chosen, in its preview and on its picture buttons. The character creator shows no outfit.
    - Done and Cancel are on every step. Done keeps the changes. Cancel and Escape leave without keeping any change. On the Outfit tab's choices view, Escape goes back to the slots view instead. From the axolotl choices card, Done or Cancel return to the Home Tank with that axolotl focused, not to the card.
    - "Surprise me" follows the F1 rule on the current tab: on a look tab it picks a different option of that tab, and on Name a different suggested name that fits the axolotl's boy or girl (F17). Nothing else changes. On the Colour, Boy or girl, Dream colour, and Outfit tabs (both Outfit views), "Surprise me" is hidden, with its place kept so Cancel and Done never move, and Tab does not reach it (open question 108). "Surprise me" never changes the colour, the boy or girl, the Dream colour, or the outfit (open question 77).

    An axolotl's real morph never changes. Only a Dream colour can be put on top of it (F14).
7. **F7** The Go exploring flow asks which owned axolotl goes exploring: the explorer. Pressing "Go exploring" in the Home Tank opens the explorer card, "Who's going exploring?" (open question 69). It shows each owned axolotl as a picture button, in its own look and outfit, with its name as the label, up to 8 to a page, with a "More axolotls" button for the next page. The chosen axolotl is marked by a ring and a tick, not by colour alone. Under the pictures is the chosen axolotl's outfit summary (F10). Three buttons follow:
    - "Go!" opens the area map (N2) with the chosen axolotl as the explorer. The trip starts when the player picks an unlocked area there, and Back on the map returns to this card with the same axolotl chosen and Go! focused (open question 116). Go! ignores presses for the first 0.8 seconds the card shows, so the press that opened the card cannot press it (open question 75).
    - "Change outfit" opens the restyle screen on the Outfit tab for the chosen axolotl (F6). Done or Cancel there returns to the card, with the same axolotl chosen.
    - "Back" closes the card and goes back to the Home Tank.

    With one owned axolotl, it is chosen already. The last explorer is remembered: choosing an axolotl saves it as the explorer (D1). When no explorer is remembered, or the remembered one is not owned, the first owned axolotl is chosen. The explorer is the axolotl the player swims as on the trip, shown with its own look and outfit.

    Text and touch targets never shrink. When the card does not fit, it makes room in this order: less padding, smaller pictures, the outfit summary rows side by side, fewer pictures per page, the title hides, the outfit summary hides, and then "More axolotls" moves beside the pictures, so the title and the summary come back if they fit. The names and the buttons never hide. The card fits the smallest canvases, 460 x 320 and 320 x 440. There, with long names and many perks, it shows 1 picture per page, with no title and no outfit summary (open question 76). "Change outfit" still shows the summary on the Outfit tab.
8. **F8** Each axolotl has eight Wearable slots:
    - Head: for example hats, flowers, and crowns.
    - Neck: for example necklaces and scarves.
    - Face: for example glasses. The eyes always stay visible.
    - Shirt: for example tank tops, T-shirts, sweaters, hoodies, and wetsuit tops. Torso only, with no sleeves on the legs, so leg loss never changes it.
    - Back: for example backpacks, bags, diving tanks, and shell packs.
    - Tail: for example bows and ribbons.
    - Socks: worn under footwear.
    - Feet: footwear, for example shoes, sneakers, boots, and flippers. One footwear item covers all four feet.

    Each Wearable fits exactly one slot. An axolotl wears at most one Wearable per slot, so items from different slots mix and match. Putting a Wearable into a filled slot takes the old one off and returns it to the Treasure Chest. Putting a Wearable on and taking it off work by touch and by keyboard, on the Outfit tab (F6). The axolotl choices card's "Outfit" opens it straight from the Home Tank (F6), and the explorer card's "Change outfit" still opens it too (F7).

    Layering goes from the body out: shirt, then neck item, then back item. An axolotl can wear socks alone, footwear alone, or both. With both, the sock shows above the shoe. When a leg is lost, its sock and shoe disappear with it. Both come back when the leg has fully regrown, never on a part-grown leg. A Socks or Feet Wearable's perk keeps working while at least one leg is left (open question 73). (Issue #16.)
9. **F9** Each Wearable has exactly one perk. The perk types are:
    - Speed: faster swimming. It raises the top speed by its percent.
    - Regrowth: lost legs grow back faster. It cuts each leg's regrowth time by its percent.
    - Dash: a longer Tail Dash. It makes each dash last longer by its percent. The recharge stays the same (open question 70).
    - Shell: blocks one hit per trip (A22).
    - Treasure sense: treasure hints appear from further away (B7).
    - Snack magnet: pulls snacks within its radius toward the explorer's head (A19; open question 71).

    A rarer Wearable has a slightly stronger perk and a fancier look. Shell perks appear only on Epic and Legendary Wearables.
10. **F10** A perk is shown as its perk icon and text, with its value, on the Wearable wherever the Wearable is listed (open question 74). Each perk type has its own icon shape. Each axolotl also has an outfit summary that shows the combined value for each perk type it wears, one row per perk type in F9 order. With no perk worn, it says "No perks yet". The outfit summary shows on the Outfit tab and on the explorer card. Perks are never shown by colour alone.
11. **F11** Each perk type has a hard total cap. When an outfit adds up past a cap, only the capped value applies, and the outfit summary shows that the cap is reached with a "max" mark after the value. Shell perks block at most one hit per trip in total, however many Shell perks the outfit has. Even a full Legendary outfit still loses a leg to every hit it does not block, and is still carried home after losing the fourth leg.
12. **F12** Perks apply only during a trip, and only from the explorer's outfit. Outfits on other axolotls have no effect. Perks never change the hit area, the telegraph, protected time, the snack eat reach, or the rule that one hit removes exactly one leg.
13. **F13** Wearables never cover the eyes, the gill roots, or a girl's cheek heart, in any slot, at any size, boy or girl, tail, body, or age, per the [art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) 6i. A girl's eyelashes count as part of the eyes. Back items (for example the Diving tank) and Head items (for example the Sun hat) may reach past the body outline, so they show as shapes against the water. Every Wearable, now and every one added later, is big and bold enough to read at a glance as what it is. In the swim view, each worn item shows at least 64 square CSS pixels of its own visible fill, at every look and every swim phase, for a Small axolotl at 320 x 440 (0.684 CSS pixels per local unit). Visible fill is the item's fill not covered by its own ink or by a later layer. Each item also meets its slot's minimum sizes (6i). No item is excepted. When an item misses a minimum, its shape, zone, or layer changes, never the minimum. Worn items use the item ink, 2.0. Shirt and Tail items draw over the near gills. The Shell necklace is a collar: a thick cord with shells along the chin, inside the body outline (issue #88; open questions 130 to 133).
14. **F14** In the Home Tank, on the restyle screen's Dream colour tab (F6), which the axolotl choices card's "Dream colour" opens directly, the player can put any owned Dream colour on any owned axolotl, and take it off at any time, by touch and by keyboard. An axolotl shows at most one Dream colour at a time. One owned Dream colour can be on any number of owned axolotls at the same time, because it is a paint, not a single item. Putting it on one axolotl never takes it off another. A Dream colour combines with spots, and the spots draw on top of it. Taking it off returns the axolotl to its real morph. A Dream colour never changes perks, the real morph, or Collection Book unlocks. Dream colours are explained by the Treasure Chest's Dream colours section line (C11) and by the first Paint shell's first-find card (C15), whose "Try it now!" opens the Dream colour tab.
15. **F15** The 16 Dream colours are told apart from each other and from the real morphs in greyscale. Each patterned or sheen Dream colour (Hearts, Tie-dye, Iridescent, Rainbow, Galaxy, and Tie-dye galaxy) has its own distinct pattern cue (for example hearts, swirls, a sheen, or stars). Solid Dream colours (Mint, Sky blue, Lavender, Sunset orange, Bubblegum, Midnight blue, Lily green, Firefly gold, Poppy red, and Plum) have no pattern cue, so they are always shown with their name label next to the swatch. On the Dream colour tab's picture buttons (F6), every label always shows and each picture button shows its swatch. Wherever a Dream colour is named in the Treasure Chest or the Collection Book, it carries the label "Dream colour — not found in nature".
16. **F16** All new screens and controls (the character creator's step screens, picture buttons, and progress dots, renaming and restyling with its tabs, the Outfit tab, the Go exploring flow's explorer card, the axolotl choices card, slots, perks, the outfit summary, the Shell sparkle, and Dream colours) work under reduced motion, can be read in greyscale, and work by touch and by keyboard, per X2, X3, and X5. On the step screens, picture buttons, and tabs:
    - The focused control has a visible focus ring.
    - The arrow keys move between picture buttons. Enter or Space picks one.
    - Tab reaches Back, Next, Surprise me (only where it shows), and Done. In restyling, Tab also reaches the tabs and Cancel, on the Dream colour tab More colours, and on the Outfit tab More slots or More wearables and, on the choices view, Back to slots. On the Outfit tab's choices view, Escape is Back to slots.
    - On the explorer card, focus starts on Go!. The arrow keys move between the axolotl pictures. Enter or Space chooses the focused axolotl or presses the focused button, ignoring key repeat. Tab reaches the pictures, More axolotls, Go!, Change outfit, and Back. Escape is Back. The card appears and leaves at once, with no motion.
    - On the axolotl choices card, focus starts on Restyle. The arrow keys, Tab, and Shift+Tab move through Restyle, Outfit, Dream colour (only where it shows), and Back, wrapping. Enter or Space presses the focused button, ignoring key repeat. Escape is Back. The card appears and leaves at once, with no motion.
    - The name box's text cursor blinks while the name box is focused. Under reduced motion it stays steady (F17).
    - The Iridescent Dream colour's sheen glides along the axolotl. Under reduced motion the sheen stays still, and it still shows.
    - Each Boy or girl picture button, in the character creator and on restyling's read-only Boy or girl screen, shows the usual boy or girl sign: a circle with an arrow for a boy, and a circle with a cross for a girl. The sign is ink on a white disc, 24 pixels across, in the lower-left corner, where the Colour step shows the morph badge. The labels "Boy" and "Girl" stay. Boy and Girl are never told apart by colour alone.
17. **F17** Names stay on this device only ([ADR 0011](../adr/0011-name-entry-stays-on-device.md)). They are never sent anywhere, and the game has no way to share them. The character creator and renaming both offer a list of suggested names, so a player never has to type a name. Suggested names fit the axolotl's boy or girl: in the character creator, in "Surprise me", and for rescued axolotls. A boy gets boy names and names for anyone. A girl gets girl names and names for anyone. "Surprise me" on the Name step, and on the Boy or girl step when the name no longer fits, picks a suggested name that fits. "Surprise me" on the Boy or girl step never changes a typed name. The lists:
    - Boy names: Axel, Finn, Ollie, Milo, and Gus.
    - Girl names: Lily, Blossom, Daisy, Rosie, and Luna.
    - Names for anyone: Bubbles, Gilly, Pebble, Noodle, Sprinkles, Mochi, Ripple, Puddle, Sunny, Peanut, Wiggles, Splash, Pickle, and Xochi.

    The name box is where the player types a name on the Name step. It looks like a place to type: a light field with an inset top and left edge, corners less rounded than the buttons, and a small pencil icon at its right end. It shows "Type a name" whenever it is empty. While it is focused, a text cursor blinks, 0.5 seconds on and 0.5 seconds off. Under reduced motion the cursor stays steady. The real text field matches the drawn name box. The name box works by touch and by keyboard.

### H. Areas, exit and stars

1. **H1** Areas are the levels of the game. The canal is Area 1, and it is the only area in the MVP (X9). After the MVP, the lily pad pond is Area 2 (built, issue #75; N2), the pond at dusk is Area 3 (built, issue #98), and the chinampa gardens are Area 4 (built, issue #76) and Area 5 (built, issue #103). Reaching an area's open exit beats it. Each area is built from its area data (Implementation Decision 8). One screen is 480 x 360 world pixels, and an area is a whole number of screens wide and tall. In an area taller than one screen, the camera scrolls up and down as well as sideways, and the zoom still fits one screen's height. The canal is 4 x 1 screens, never scrolls up or down, and plays exactly as before. The pond is 5 x 1 screens and never scrolls up or down either. It is harder than the canal: wider, with more tilapia, more snacks, faster snack drift, and a hazard (N2, N3). A one-leg axolotl can swim it to the exit and home in under 180 seconds. Area 3 is 6 x 1 screens and never scrolls up or down either. It is a little harder than Area 2: wider, with more snacks, faster snack drift, darting snacks, a stronger stream, more treasure slots, and two herons (N2, N3). A one-leg axolotl can swim it to the exit and home in under 180 seconds too. Area 4 is 6 x 2 screens, the first area taller than one screen, so the camera follows the axolotl up and down as well as sideways, by touch (the swim pad) and by keyboard (the arrow keys and WASD). It is harder than Area 3: twice as tall, with more snacks, faster snack drift, more treasure slots, a tighter tilapia (the predator ladder), and the ghost nets (N2, N3). A one-leg axolotl can swim it to the exit and home, each way in under 180 seconds. Area 5 is 7 x 2 screens and scrolls up and down as Area 4 does. It is the first U-turn area: the way runs right along the top row, down in the rightmost screen column, and back left along the bottom row to the exit under the Home Spot (N2). It is harder than Area 4: wider, with more snacks, faster snack drift, more treasure slots, a tighter tilapia (the predator ladder), two garter snakes, and the water hyacinth mats (N2, N3). A one-leg axolotl can swim it to the exit, waiting at each mat channel while it is closed, and home, each way in under 180 seconds: measured along the main path, 76.3 seconds to the exit when it sets off at the mats' time 0, and 75.3 to 81.9 seconds over 16 start times through the mat cycle (open question 234).
2. **H2** Each area has one clear, reachable exit: the canal's at the far end of its main swim path, the pond's at its right end, past the stream, Area 3's at its right end, past the stream and the root arch, out of every heron's reach, Area 4's deep in the bottom right, past the gap current, out of the last tilapia's strike and clear of every ghost net, and Area 5's in the bottom left, straight under the Home Spot, at the end of the way back along the bottom row, out of every predator's reach and clear of every ghost net, mat sweep, and hiding spot (issue #103; open question 222). When the exit is open, its sign's arrow points toward the nearer side edge of the area: right in Areas 1 to 4, and left in Area 5, whose exit is in the left half (open question 224). It stays closed until every snack in the area has been eaten. Closed and open differ by shape or icon, not by colour alone, including under reduced motion.
3. **H3** A snack counter (an icon and a number) shows the snacks left. When only 3 are left, each gets the same hint sparkle that hidden treasures use (B7; see open question 16). The sparkle is static under reduced motion.
4. **H4** Snacks move. In Area 1 they drift slowly. In Area 2 they drift a little faster, in Area 3 a little faster again, about 15.2 pixels per second at most, in Area 4 a little faster still, about 15.6 pixels per second at most (issue #76), and in Area 5 a little faster again, about 15.8 pixels per second at most (issue #103). In every area the drift stays slower than a one-leg axolotl swims against the area's strongest stream and at most half the Snack magnet pull (see Tuning values). A snack is eaten where it is drawn, drift and bob included, by the axolotl's head (A19). In some areas 3 snacks also dart away a little, once each, by the same shared rule, the one-time darters (built, issues #98, #76, and #103; N2 growth). In an area with freshwater shrimp, the shrimp replace the one-time darters; each area's N2 paragraph says which it has (open question 274). The one-time darter rule:
    - Which: the darters are set by the area data, spread evenly through the area's worms and bugs in list order, with no random draw, so every trip has the same darters (open question 163). A tadpole or a shrimp is never a one-time darter (open question 274).
    - When: a darter darts when the head reach centre comes within 60 pixels of it, where it is drawn, drift and bob included. That is outside the 36 pixel eat reach, so it darts before it can be eaten.
    - The hop: one short hop of 40 pixels, straight away from the head, gliding at 120 pixels per second, slower than the axolotl swims. Under reduced motion it lands at once.
    - Once: each darter darts at most once per trip, then drifts and bobs from where it landed and stays catchable, so the exit can always open (H2). The Snack magnet still pulls it.
    - Where not: a darter never hops where its drift and bob could carry it out of the swim bounds, up to the surface, into the Home Spot ring or the safe area, or into a current, or within 80 pixels of a plastic worm, or, in an area with water hyacinth mats, where it could overlap a mat pair's sweep (open question 228). Then it does not dart at that moment, and it can dart later.

    Mobile snacks (owner decision on issue #117, 2026-10-23; built as shared parts in issue #117, opt-in, and in no shipped area yet; [ADR 0021](../adr/0021-danger-pass-chase-rule-and-moving-hazards.md); open questions 269 to 278): two snack types move on their own, so snacks get harder to catch area by area (the snack ramp below; N2, the danger pass). Worms and bugs keep drifting and bobbing as before. The shared rules:
    - Tadpole, a swimmer (open question 271): it swims there and back at a steady speed along a short straight lane, from its start (x, y) to (x + dx, y + dy), 80 to 160 pixels long, and turns at once at each end. Its speed is set per area, above 0 and at most 72 pixels per second, 0.8 times a one-leg axolotl's 90, so a one-leg axolotl always swims faster than any tadpole. It comes back to each end of its lane every 2 x length / speed seconds (4 seconds on a 120 pixel lane at 60 pixels per second), so waiting at an end always catches it. An area's tadpoles start out of step. It has no drift and no bob, and its drawn place is the same under full and reduced motion. It faces the way it swims. Its wiggle is drawing only: 4 tail frames at 8 frames a second and a body sway of up to 8 degrees each way; under reduced motion it shows its first frame with no sway and slides along its lane.
    - Freshwater shrimp, a repeat darter (open question 272): at rest it drifts and bobs like a worm or a bug, facing the head. It is ready at the trip start. A ready shrimp, drawn within its trigger radius of the head reach centre (inclusive, but not exactly on it), flicks straight away from the head, backward, by its flick distance, gliding at its flick speed; under reduced motion it lands at once. It then rests for its rest time, counted only on swimming steps, and is ready again, with no limit on flicks. It flicks only where its drift and bob at the landing stay in a clear place (below); otherwise it stays ready and does not flick that moment. A collected shrimp never moves, and a flick whose shrimp is eaten stops. The Snack magnet may pull a gliding shrimp, and the two moves add up. Limits: a trigger radius above the 36 pixel eat reach; a rest of at least 1 second; a flick of 24 to 72 pixels; a flick speed above 0 and at most 150 pixels per second, a four-leg axolotl's top speed; and the catch limit, rest x 70 ≥ trigger radius + flick distance − 36, so a one-leg axolotl that swims straight at a shrimp that has just landed reaches it within one rest (70 pixels per second is a one-leg axolotl's 90 less 20 for drift).
    - Where (open question 273): a tadpole's whole lane, and a shrimp's whole drift and bob box, at its start and at every flick landing, stay inside the swim bounds and below the highest wave; outside the Home Spot ring, the safe area, every current, and every mat pair's whole sweep, edges counting as inside; at least 80 pixels from every plastic worm where it is now, every hazard's shape (a drifting ghost net by its whole drift path), every element sweep, and every moving hazard's sweep; and farther than the strike range S of every threat for the risk ladder (B10): a predator's lunge range from its patrol loop, with no leash added, and a hazard's reach. A spat plastic worm never lands within 80 pixels of a tadpole lane: it stays where it was, as when its landing is in a current. The Snack magnet may still pull any snack toward the head (A19).
    - Data (open question 270): mobile snacks are fixed area data, each tadpole lane and shrimp start written out as numbers in the area's own layout, never seeded. They come after the seeded worms and bugs, tadpoles first, then shrimp, so the seeded snacks keep their places. An area keeps its total snack count: its worms and bugs go down by its mobile count. The snacks that start in a current are always worms or bugs, because a mobile snack never enters a current.
    - Counting (open question 274): a tadpole and a shrimp each count as one snack, like a worm or a bug, for the counter, the last-3 sparkle (H3), the exit (H2), the snack speed-up (A19), and the reset when carried home (A12), so the exit can always open. Mobile snacks are snacks, not hazards, predators, or elements, so neither the one-new-thing rule nor the danger bar counts them (N2).
    - Fact cards (open question 276): "Meet the tadpole" and "Freshwater shrimp" (C13), each found the first time the axolotl's centre comes within 120 pixels of an uncollected snack of that type, where it is drawn, outside the safe area, and shown with play paused, like A21.

    Snack ramp (owner decision on issue #117, 2026-10-23; open question 278): from Area 2 on, every area has both mobile snack types, and by area number the mobile share, the tadpole speed, the shrimp flick distance and flick speed never go down, and the shrimp rest never goes up. Area 1 has no mobile snacks. Each area's issue proves its own row in its own layout test, by the check range in the share and tadpole speed columns. The values are starting points for the phase 2 issues (N2, the danger pass), to tune in play-testing; no shipped area has mobile snacks yet. Every shrimp's trigger radius is 60 pixels. A phase 2 issue that changes a value records it in its own N2 paragraph and a new open question, never in this table.

    | Area | Mobile share (check range) | Snacks: tadpoles + shrimp + worms + bugs (worms + bugs today) | Tadpole speed, pixels per second (check range) | Shrimp rest, flick distance, flick speed | Snack magnet pull |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | 1 | 0%, none | 16: 0 + 0 + 10 + 6 (10 + 6) | None | None | 40 |
    | 2 | About 20%: 20% (15 to 25%) | 20: 2 + 2 + 10 + 6 (12 + 8) | 25 (20 to 30) | 2.0 seconds, 36 pixels, 100 pixels per second | 40 (1.2 x 25 = 30 is lower) |
    | 3 | About 35%: 33% (30 to 40%) | 24: 4 + 4 + 10 + 6 (14 + 10) | 35 (30 to 40) | 1.7 seconds, 44 pixels, 115 pixels per second | 42 |
    | 4 | About 50%: 50% (45 to 55%) | 28: 7 + 7 + 8 + 6 (16 + 12) | 48 (40 to 55) | 1.4 seconds, 52 pixels, 130 pixels per second | 57.6 |
    | 5 | About 60%: 59% (55 to 65%) | 32: 10 + 9 + 7 + 6 (18 + 14) | 60 (55 to 65) | 1.2 seconds, 60 pixels, 145 pixels per second | 72 |
    | 6 on | Rising to a cap of about 70%: at least the area before's (55 to 70%) | Its own | At least the area before's (60 to 72) | Area 5's or harder, inside the catch limit, with a flick speed of 145 to 150 pixels per second | max(40, 1.2 x its fastest mobile snack) |

5. **H5** Reaching the open exit banks every trip find at once, as at the Home Spot (A14). Then a kind "Area beaten" screen shows the stars earned (see open question 17).
6. **H6** Each area has up to 3 stars:
    - Star 1: reach the exit.
    - Star 2: find the area's special treasure (see open question 13).
    - Star 3: rescue the stranded axolotl, or finish the trip without losing a leg. A hit blocked by a Shell perk costs no leg (A22), so it does not lose this star.

    Stars are awarded when the area is beaten. An area's special treasure is always Common or Rare, never Epic or Legendary. The canal's is the Toy boat (Rare), the pond's is the Frog statue (Rare; issue #75), Area 3's is the Wooden duck decoy (Rare; issue #98), Area 4's is the Scarecrow (Rare; issue #76), and Area 5's is the Piñata (Rare; issue #103). The Castle tower, the canal's special treasure before, stays owned by everyone who banked it, and is now an ordinary Epic that turns up by the normal chance (B4) (issue #93; open question 136).
7. **H7** A star, once earned, is kept forever. When an area's special treasure changes, stars already earned are kept: a player who earned Star 2 with the Castle tower keeps it. A player who already banked the Toy boat earns Star 2 the next time they beat Area 1. From now on, Star 2 in Area 1 needs the Toy boat (issue #93). Any unlocked area can be replayed from the area map (N2). Replaying an area can earn the stars still missing, and hiding spots shuffle on replays too (B4). Stars per area are saved (D2) and shown, including each area's best stars on the area map.
8. **H8** The exit, the snack counter, the last-snack sparkle, and the "Area beaten" screen work by touch and by keyboard, under reduced motion, and in greyscale, per X2, X3, and X5.

### X. Cross-cutting

1. **X1** Nothing is scary or gory. The tilapia is round and cartoony with no frightening face, per the [art style guide](../art-style-guide.md). UI text never uses "die", "dead", "killed", or "game over".
2. **X2** Rarity, legs, the telegraph, the protected state, and locked or unfound items can all be read in greyscale.
3. **X3** Every new animation (poof, regrowth, telegraph, carried home, tank swimming, and celebrations) has a still or minimal version under reduced motion. Gameplay still works.
4. **X4** There is no flashing beyond the art style guide limits, and no screen shake.
5. **X5** New touch targets are at least 48 by 48 CSS pixels. Primary actions are at least 64 pixels tall. The keyboard can do everything touch can.
6. **X6** Text sizes follow the art style guide. Wording is readable by an 8-year-old.
7. **X7** There are no ads, chat, purchases, analytics, accounts, or third-party requests, per [ADR 0007](../adr/0007-kid-safety-and-privacy.md).
8. **X8** Swim-test features keep working: the swim pad, the Dash button, keyboard controls, snacks, the current, reeds, bubbles, and pause.
9. **X9** The world is made of named areas. A habitat is a group of one or more areas with its own look, and each area is in one habitat (N2). The MVP ships one area: the canal, which is Area 1 (H1), in the canal habitat. After the MVP, the lily pad pond habitat adds Area 2 (built, issue #75) and Area 3, the pond at dusk (built, issue #98; N2), and the chinampa gardens habitat adds Area 4 (built, issue #76) and Area 5 (built, issue #103), so five areas ship in three habitats. An area may use its own variant of its habitat's look, such as Area 3's dusk look (open question 168). The game must allow more areas to be added later and joined, so a player can swim from one to another within a single trip. Adding areas must not change the rules for legs, banking, treasures, or saves. Saved progress identifies treasures and hiding spots per area, so new areas never break old saves.
10. **X10** In-trip messages (the find and rescue toasts, the snack banner, the first-trip hint, the plastic worm message "Bleh! That was plastic, not food.", and the ghost net message "Snagged on an old net! Wriggle free.", both of which use the snack banner's placement, N3) never cover the controls or open water in the middle of the play area. They show at the top or bottom edge of the screen, else over the Trip Bag panel, else over the status panels. The middle is the full width, from 30% to 70% of the way down the water as drawn on screen (from the water line to the sea bed, not the screen height). Inside it a message may cover only the status panels themselves (the snack counter, legs, and Trip Bag), never the water beside or between them. Outside it a message may go anywhere except over a control. When the snack banner or the first-trip hint has no such place, it tries every place again with smaller text, 20 pixels (still at least the 18 pixel minimum, X6), before it waits; the find toast never shrinks (open question 58). A message with no such place waits, hidden, until one opens up. At 460 x 320 and 460 x 340 with Go home showing, only the "Tie-dye galaxy Paint shell" and "Rainbow dragonfly wings" find toasts wait this way (open questions 38 and 154). The canal, the pond, and Area 3 never scroll up or down, so each screen size has fixed water rows. An area taller than one screen, such as Area 4, scrolls up and down (H1). There the middle is measured on screen, from the water line or the screen top, whichever is lower, to the sea bed or the screen bottom, whichever is higher (open question 121). The messages move again when that water moves by 8 CSS pixels or more, or 0.1 seconds after a smaller move.
11. **X11** Frame time stays within the budget in [ADR 0013](../adr/0013-axolotl-rig-and-frame-budget.md) in the four measured cases: the canal with the explorer and a rescue, the Home Tank with four axolotls, the title screen, and the dev test area (taller than one screen, with the explorer and a rescue, via `?area=dev-test-area` in a dev build), each on WebGL and on Canvas (open question 106).

## Implementation Decisions

1. **Two-tier ownership.** Finds live in one of two tiers. The Trip Bag holds unbanked finds for this trip only. The banked tier holds the Treasure Chest and the axolotls in the Home Tank. Only banking moves finds from the Trip Bag to the banked tier. Being carried home returns Trip Bag finds to the world. No gameplay event removes anything banked. Only "Start over" does.
2. **Stable identities.** The treasure catalogue and hiding spots are fixed per area. Every catalogue item, hiding spot, and morph has a stable, unique identity. Saves therefore survive updates and additions to the catalogue or to areas. Which spot holds which treasure is picked fresh each trip and is never saved.
3. **Persistent save contents.** The save is one versioned JSON save of the hub only, kept in `localStorage` under the key `glam-gills:save` ([ADR 0012](../adr/0012-save-on-device.md)). The hub is banked state, the tank layout, each axolotl's look and outfit, the chosen explorer, Collection Book unlocks, stars earned per area, and settings. Nothing from a trip is saved. It stays on the device.
    - Upgrade chain: older saves are upgraded forward, one upgrade per version step, then loaded.
    - Additive rule: a new hub field loads with its default when an older save lacks it, with no version bump. Only a change an old save cannot load by default (a rename, a new meaning) adds an upgrade and bumps the save version.
    - Repair rule: a save that is structurally damaged (not JSON, not an object, no valid version, no hub, or an upgrade that fails) or from a newer version is unreadable. Anything else loads, repaired field by field: unknown ids are dropped and bad fields fall back to their defaults, so one bad field never wipes the rest.
4. **Deterministic rules.** The rules for legs, regrowth, protected time, the tilapia telegraph, lunge and cooldown, the heron's wading, warning, stab, and cooldown, cover from a heron, darting snacks, banking, hiding difficulty by rarity, the snack regrowth speed-up, the exit rule, star rules, save upgrades, and the update decision are deterministic. They can be tested without rendering, per [ADR 0006](../adr/0006-testing-strategy.md).
5. **Fail state.** Fail-state behaviour follows [ADR 0008](../adr/0008-gentle-fail-state.md), as amended by [ADR 0010](../adr/0010-carried-home-full-reset.md).
6. **Update check.** Any update check is first-party only and sends no player data.
7. **Tuning numbers.** Tuning numbers are adjustable starting points, not part of the contract.
8. **Areas as data.** Each area is described as data: its layout, look, hazards, hiding spots, treasures, snacks, exit, and special treasure. New habitats can be added without rewriting game rules. Moving from one area to another is a world feature, not a special case inside one area. Built in the area framework (issue #73, [ADR 0014](../adr/0014-area-framework.md)):
    - Each area's data holds its id, its number, its habitat, and its size in screens (wide x tall). Its layout holds the start, the Home Spot safe area, the reed beds, the cave and current hiding spots, the currents, the cave, the main swim path, the stranding places, its treasures, its snack counts, and its pads. It also holds its tilapia routes, its other predator routes (each naming its species), its hazards, and its timing or puzzle elements. The area also names its exit, its special treasure, and how its snacks drift, and it may name its own look and which of its snacks dart (issue #98).
    - The game builds each area's layout from its data and a seed. The built layout also has the area's size, its sea bed top, and its number of screens across and down.
    - A habitat is data too: an id, a name, and a look.
    - Predator species and hazard types are data (N3). The tilapia keeps its own column of routes beside the other predator routes (open question 125).
    - Areas only for development and tests are never in the area list and never ship (open question 122).
    - The lily pad pond, Area 2, is built as data on the framework (issue #75). Its one new rule set is the plastic worm's, a hazard that only slows (N3).
    - The pond at dusk, Area 3, is built as data on the framework (issue #98). It adds the heron, the second predator species on the shared predator rules, with two herons that wade near the surface and warn on sight (N3, issue #111). Its species data turns on three shared rule options, each absent on the tilapia, so the tilapia plays exactly as before: hunting by sight, so it cannot see an axolotl in cover; a strike spot fixed at the warning start; and standing still through its cooldown (open questions 207, 210, and 213). The encounter takes the area's cover (its reed patches, pads, and cave) from the area's layout. Area 3 has no timing element: issue #111 removed the heron's shadow cycle, the timer-started warning that issue #98 built, and no species uses a timer now. The generic timing element data stays for later areas (open question 217). Area 3's look is a variant of the pond's look, `pond-dusk`, named in its area data (`AreaData.look`). The art layer reads each look's properties (its sky and wash, the stream, the kind of cave, the pond props, and the fireflies) and never checks a look id (open question 168). Its darting snacks are area data too (H4).
    - The chinampa gardens, Area 4, are built as data on the framework (issue #76; [ADR 0018](../adr/0018-chinampa-gardens-ghost-nets-and-predator-ladder.md)), in a new habitat, `chinampa`, with its own look, `chinampa`. Its one new rule set is the ghost net's, a hazard that only slows (N3). Its layout may also hold, as optional data that older areas leave out, so they play exactly as before: its own tilapia tuning (the predator ladder, N2; absent means the shared tilapia tuning), and its overhanging banks with the hiding spots under them (the bank cover, B5; never built from the bank art's columns). The chinampa look adds its own properties too (the garden plot banks, the root tangle cave, the gap edges either side of a current, and the chinampa props), read like the others.
    - The chinampa gardens' Area 5 is built as data on the framework (issue #103; [ADR 0020](../adr/0020-u-turn-areas-garter-snake-and-hyacinth-mats.md)), in the chinampa habitat with the habitat's own look. It adds the garter snake, the third predator species, and the water hyacinth mats, the first timing element to ship and the first thing that blocks the explorer (N3). It generalises the framework for a U-turn area (N2, the area shape rule): the safe area is area data and need not be the Home Spot's whole column; the side past a current comes from the current's own flow, not from where home is (B6); and the open exit sign's arrow points toward the nearer side edge of the area (H2). An element type may carry a fact card and a meet range as optional data, and a mat pair spawn its geometry; elements without them play exactly as before.
9. **Per-axolotl data.** Every owned axolotl has a stable, unique identity. Its look (real morph, applied Dream colour, boy or girl, face, gill style, spots, tail, body, age, size, and name) and its outfit are saved per axolotl, against that identity. Wearables and Dream colours are referred to by their stable catalogue identities. Live axolotls are a rig of baked parts ([ADR 0013](../adr/0013-axolotl-rig-and-frame-budget.md), issue #46). Every part re-bakes when its appearance key changes: every look field (boy or girl, tail, body, age, size, and the rest), the morph colours and Dream colour, the outfit, and the bake scale. Each leg also re-bakes when its own leg state changes. A pose never re-bakes. Picture-button previews are still bakes, repainted only when the look or the outfit shown changes.
10. **Perks as data.** Each Wearable's perk type and value are data. Perks are modifiers applied on top of the base rules for speed, regrowth, Tail Dash, hits, hints, and snacks. Each perk type has a total cap, and only the capped total applies. Perk and cap rules are deterministic and can be tested without rendering.
11. **Looks never change play.** Size, boy or girl, tail, body, age, face, gill style, spots, morph, and Dream colour never change the hit area or speed. Only perks change play.
12. **Set-ready Wearables.** Each Wearable may carry an optional set identity in its data, so Wearable sets (N5) can be added later without reworking the catalogue or saves. The MVP has no sets and no set bonuses.

## Testing Decisions

- **Headless rule tests** prove the deterministic rules, per [ADR 0006](../adr/0006-testing-strategy.md). They cover:
  - leg state changes;
  - exactly one leg lost per hit;
  - the protected window;
  - regrowth timing, including during pause;
  - top speed decreasing as legs are lost;
  - beating every current with one leg;
  - at most one tilapia telegraphing at a time, and a 1-leg axolotl reaching the Home Spot from anywhere with every tilapia patrolling;
  - shared predator rules from species data (N3): the tilapia as the first species, playing as before with its tests unchanged; a warning starting only within range, outside the safe area, and when the species allows it; the warning lasting its wind-up, the strike following the aim locked at its start, then the cooldown; and, with a test species beside the tilapia, at most one predator warning or striking at a time across every species (A1, open question 118). The shared rule options (issue #111; `src/logic/predators.test.ts`), each absent on the tilapia: with sight cover, no warning at a target in cover, a warning whose target is in cover at the end-of-warning re-check cancelled, cover re-checked only at that end, and a strike never connecting with a target in cover, while a species without the option ignores cover; with a fixed strike spot, the spot fixed at the warning start and stabbed from where the predator stands, missing a target that moved and connecting once the stab tip comes within the hit reach of a target that stayed, the stab tip worked out from the state alone in a straight line over the strike, and no strike spot ever on a predator without the option; and with a still cooldown, standing still facing the target through the cooldown, then patrolling again, while a predator without the option swims its route;
  - the wading heron (N3, issue #111; `src/logic/predators.test.ts`): its species data, the second species, a sight hunter that stabs a fixed spot, stands still to cool down, and never bumps; its tuning (a 1.2 second warning, above the 0.8 second floor and longer than the tilapia's, a 110 pixel stab in 0.3 seconds at the Medium hit reach, a 3 second cooldown, and a slow wade); only the shared sight-based phase events; wading its stretch back and forth at its wading speed while the axolotl is out of reach; the warning on sight starting on the first step the axolotl is within reach of where the heron stands, inclusive, measured from where it stands and not from its stretch, so it warns once it wades near enough; never warning at an axolotl below its reach, in the safe area (edges included), or in cover, where it wades on instead, nor while another predator is warning or striking (A1); standing still for the warning with its facing following the axolotl, and the strike spot fixed at the warning start; the stab hitting an axolotl that stayed once the tip is within the hit reach, the tip moving in a straight line from where the heron stands to the spot over 0.3 seconds, and never connecting with an axolotl in cover, even at the spot; a warning cancelled into the 3 second cooldown when the axolotl hides in cover or swims out of reach before it ends; standing still facing the axolotl for the 3 second cooldown after a stab, hit or miss, or a cancel, then wading on; and freezing in every phase on zero, negative, or non-finite steps; and the route loop distance, the wrap segment included, matching the hiding rules' route distance exactly;
  - the heron in play (N3, issue #111): in the encounter, the tilapia first, then each heron route with the heron species and its own tuning; never bumping, even with the axolotl on its strike spot, whatever its touch reach (open question 170); a connected stab from where the heron stands as one hit, costing one leg and starting protected time and the bump cooldown; a Shell perk blocking the first stab; the fourth stab carrying the axolotl home; its cover taken from the zone data, so it never warns at an axolotl in reeds, under a pad, or in the cave, but warns and stabs in open water, while the tilapia ignores cover; a warning cancelled into the cooldown when the axolotl hides before it ends; a stab at the spot fixed at the warning start, a hit when the axolotl stays and a miss when it moved; the cover geometry copied and kept on reset; only one predator warning or striking at a time across two herons and a tilapia (A1); and the heron frozen while paused (`src/logic/encounter.test.ts`). On a trip, a heron met from the first step within its 180 pixel meet range of where it stands, and not past that, wherever it has waded to; nothing met inside the safe area; a heron met with the axolotl in cover, because cover stops a warning, not a meeting; and the cover data kept on the next trip (`src/logic/trip.test.ts`; open question 216). Its strike zone for B10: the heron route's strike range S is its species lunge range, 110, banded by the shared bands; it warns at a sampled point, standing on the nearest point of its stretch, exactly when that point is in the strike zone and not in cover, the zone edge counting as in reach, never from anywhere on its stretch outside the zone, in deep water below it, or in the safe area; and it never warns at a covered point under a pad, in the root arch, or in reeds, though the risk ladder still counts that point as near (`src/logic/hiding.test.ts`; open questions 207 and 215). The heron fact card's id, name, title, and body, with no banned words and a meet range larger than its reach (`src/logic/factCards.test.ts`);
  - hazard hits (N3, open question 126): touching a hazard that costs a leg, within its reach of the shape edge, costs one leg, starts protected time (A5), is blocked by an unused Shell perk (A22), carries the axolotl home on the fourth leg (A11), and leaves the bump cooldown unchanged; a hazard that only slows never hits; and timing elements repeat on their cycle;
  - plastic worms (N3, issue #75): slow-only, with the snack head reach, its meet range, and its fact card, never a threat and never costing a leg; touched by the head, not the body centre, within the reach, inclusive; eating one starts 3 seconds of queasy time and spits only that worm 48 pixels past the head reach centre, clamped to the swim bounds, or leaves it where it was when that landing is inside a current or the safe area; no stacking and no timer reset while queasy; a spat worm slowing again once queasy time ends; queasy time counting down to 0 and ending at once; only the top speed scaled while queasy (54 pixels per second with one leg), so Tail Dash is not slowed; and the wobble peaking at its amplitude, 0 under reduced motion and when not queasy;
  - the lily pad pond (Area 2, issue #75): 5 x 1 screens from the Home Spot; 20 snacks, 6 plastic worms, 4 tilapia, and 7 treasure slots; the same fixed content for every seed; every spot matching its labels, B6 and B10 included, and clear of every turn; a pool for each rarity at least as big as its pond treasures; the Common and Rare lily pad spots; spots, the exit, and the tilapia routes in the water and out of the safe area, with every pair of routes more than twice the lunge range apart and a far side to the stream; plastic worms out of the safe area and the Home Spot ring, farther than a lunge from every tilapia route, never under a lily pad, and far enough from every current that queasy time ends before the axolotl reaches it; snacks drifting slower than a one-leg axolotl swims against the strongest current and at most half the magnet pull; harder than the canal (wider, more tilapia, more snacks, faster drift, and a hazard); and a one-leg axolotl swimming the main path, through the stream, to the exit and back home;
  - the pond golden test (`src/logic/pondGolden.test.ts`, issue #98): Area 2's built layout, its trip placements over every zone seed and trip seeds 1 to 25, with banked and returned treasures too, and the encounter and trip traces (met, legs lost, and phase) along a fixed scripted swim of at least 90 seconds that parks inside every tilapia lunge range, match a saved snapshot, the same on every run. So a shared rule changed for Area 3 cannot quietly change Area 2. The placement snapshots with three banked treasures and with two returned treasures were saved again for the one-step fallback (B4, issue #111); the layout projections and both traces did not change;
  - the lily pad pond at dusk (Area 3, issue #98; `src/logic/pond2Layout.test.ts`): 6 x 1 screens from the Home Spot; 24 snacks, 5 plastic worms, 4 tilapia, two herons, and 8 treasure slots; exactly two herons, `heron-0` and `heron-1`, each on a two-point stretch, and no timing element (issue #111); the same fixed content for every seed; every spot matching its labels, B6 and B10 for every threat included, and a pool for each rarity at least as big as its Area 3 treasures; the Common and Rare pad spots out of every heron's reach; every Epic and Legendary spot in a heron's strike zone, in the root arch or past the stream, every Common and Rare spot farther than a heron's reach from every stretch, and every spot at least the turn clearance from every heron waypoint; the root arch hanging from just under the surface, inside the swim bounds, above the deepest point a heron can warn at, and under `heron-1`, beyond the stream, the spots past it, and `heron-0`; every pair of tilapia routes more than twice the lunge range apart; the tilapia routes and the heron stretches in the water and out of the safe area; each heron stretch at y 120 in the back half, 120 to 200 pixels long, with a 110 pixel reach to y 230, and a deep lane below y 230 under each stretch that every heron and tilapia leave open; a far side to the stream; a stream a little stronger than Area 2's that one leg still beats; every stranding place 80 pixels from every route, out of every heron's reach, and clear of spots, currents, and the cave;   - the fairness test (B10, open question 81), in the canal, in the pond, in Area 3, in Area 4, and in Area 5 (one file per area since issue #117, `src/logic/canalFairness.test.ts`, `src/logic/pondFairness.test.ts`, `src/logic/pond2Fairness.test.ts`, `src/logic/chinampaFairness.test.ts`, and `src/logic/chinampa2Fairness.test.ts`, on the shared harness `src/logic/riskFairness.ts`; open question 264): every spot a treasure can hide in can be reached and left with no hit, for some start time in the patrol, with four legs and the real encounter rules; start times every 0.25 seconds over the longest patrol loop, straight swims at base top speed from the nearest point outside lunge range, and one Tail Dash only into a Legendary's pot. In Area 3 (issue #111), the safe starts are also out of every heron's reach of its stretch; the start times run over the longer of the longest tilapia loop and the longest heron wade there and back along its stretch (18 seconds); the axolotl parks long enough for any warning and stab (at least 1.5 seconds); an axolotl waiting at each Legendary pot, or, for a pot in cover, at the last uncovered point on its straight way in that a heron can reach, is hit, so the check is never empty (open question 219); every spot keeps the turn clearance from every tilapia and heron waypoint; and other seeds keep every spot, route, and hazard the same. In Area 4, the check plays Area 4's own tilapia tuning (its patrol speed, its 145 pixel strike, and its own strike time), bands every spot by S = 145, catches a hit when the axolotl waits at each Legendary pot, and holds on the default seed and on other seeds, which keep every spot, route, net, and the tilapia tuning the same. In Area 5 (issue #103), the check plays Area 5's own tilapia tuning and both garter snakes: the start times run over the longer of the longest tilapia loop and the longest snake swim there and back along its stretch, and the axolotl parks long enough for the longer strike; every spot is banded by S = 150 for the tilapia and S = 110 for the snakes; the safe starts are outside Area 5's lunge range and every snake's reach; it catches a hit when the axolotl waits at each Legendary pot; every spot keeps the turn clearance from every tilapia and snake waypoint; and it holds on the default seed and on other seeds, which keep every spot, route, net, and the tilapia tuning the same;
  - save round-trip: the hub saved and loaded again comes back exactly, and only hub fields are saved;
  - an unreadable save: damaged text starts fresh and is kept under `glam-gills:save-unreadable` (the first one kept) until the next "Start over"; a newer-version save starts fresh, is left untouched, and turns saving off; anything else is repaired field by field, dropping unknown ids and defaulting bad fields;
  - upgrading saves from older save versions through the upgrade chain, a failing upgrade counting as damaged, and a new field loading with its default with no version bump;
  - the first-find cards (C15, D2): none due with nothing banked, each kind due once one is banked and its card not seen, none once seen, the Decoration, Wearable, Paint shell order, and a fresh session (Start over) with none seen; the "Try it now!" axolotl being the explorer, else the first owned axolotl, and restyle starting on the Dream colour tab; and the saved seen kinds: encode, decode and repair (a missing field or one that is not an array loads as none seen, unknown kinds dropped, each kind once), with no save version bump, and an older save loading with none seen;
  - first-meeting fact cards (A21, C14, N3): a hazard or predator met within its meet range, never inside the safe area, each id once, in meet order; the first met card not yet seen coming due; the tilapia card coming due on the same step as before (meet range 180); and the Collection Book listing the fact cards in area-table order, found when met, with a picture only with Book art, then "Boy or girl?"; the plastic worm and heron cards' exact titles and bodies; the heron card found only once the heron is met, never with a picture, and listed after the plastic worm card; the ghost net card found only once a ghost net is met, never with a picture, listed after the heron card, with "Explore the chinampa gardens to find this card." while unfound (issue #76); the garter snake and water hyacinth cards found only once met, never with a picture, listed after the ghost net card, with "Explore the chinampa gardens to find this card." while unfound (issue #103); an element card's hint naming the habitat of the first area whose elements have it; the refuge cards after the area cards, so the book counts 9 fact cards; each unfound card's hint naming the habitat of the first area where its hazard, predator, or element appears ("Explore the lily pad pond to find this card." for the heron); the book at 1 row a page in 8 + 16 + 9 pages; and the "Collection Book" title hiding only on a featured page whose entry does not fit under it, measured on every supported canvas: only the found plastic worm, heron, and water hyacinth pages, at the canvases listed in C14 (`src/logic/collectionBook.test.ts`); and the garter snake and water hyacinth cards' ids, titles, and bodies, and every area fact card fitting every supported canvas, the heron and water hyacinth bodies wrapping at the tight-padding width at 320 x 440 (`src/logic/factCards.test.ts`, `src/logic/cardCopyFit.test.ts`);

  - "Surprise me": on the creator's Colour step a valid whole new axolotl, with boy or girl set and a suggested name that fits it; on every other step only that step's choice changing, always to a different choice, with nothing else changing; and on the hidden restyle tabs (Colour, Boy or girl, Dream colour, and Outfit) doing nothing;

  - suggested names fitting boy or girl, and a rescued axolotl getting its seeded boy or girl and the first unused name that fits;
  - the name box: the "Type a name" placeholder whenever it is empty, the cursor timing (0.5 seconds on and 0.5 seconds off, steady under reduced motion), and room for the pencil at its right end;
  - he or she text: text about one axolotl matches its boy or girl and never says "they" or "it";
  - outfit and slot rules: one Wearable per slot, each Wearable in one slot only, and on at most one axolotl at a time;
  - keep-clear zones for every slot: each worn Wearable, in all eight slots, at every size, boy or girl, tail, body, and age, and every swim phase, never covers the eyes (eyelashes included), the gill roots, or a girl's cheek heart, using the keep-clear zones as revised in the [art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) 6i, the cheek heart's zone included, and Socks and Feet draw only on a leg that is present;
  - the swim-view floor (F13): each worn Wearable's visible fill, the fill not covered by its own ink or by a later layer, is at least 64 square CSS pixels at every look and swim phase, for a Small axolotl at 320 x 440 (0.684 CSS pixels per local unit). No item is excepted;
  - the slot minimums (F13): each Wearable meets its slot's minimum sizes on every look, in all eight slots (Head, Neck, Face, Shirt, Back, Tail, Socks, and Feet). The numbers are in `WEARABLE_SLOT_MINIMUMS` (`src/art/wearableFit.ts`) and the [art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) 6i;
  - the durable size rule (F13): one suite reads every Wearable in the whole catalogue (`ALL_TREASURES`, the canal's then the pond's), so each new Wearable is held to the same rule. It fails when a Wearable has no art, or art has no catalogue entry. It fails when any Wearable falls below the floor or its slot minimums. It fails when the recorded per-item table (`WEARABLE_RECORD`) is missing an item, or lists one that is not in the catalogue. A later area's Wearables must be added to `ALL_TREASURES`;

- **Manual phone play-tests** on the GitHub Pages build, per [ADR 0006](../adr/0006-testing-strategy.md), cover what rules cannot: feel, telegraph readability, a non-scary tone, a greyscale check, reduced motion, touch target sizes, and whether the full reset when carried home feels too harsh (open question 15). They also cover the area map (N2: the flow from Go! through the map to the trip, Back to the explorer card, a locked area's message, drag and wheel scrolling, and reduced motion) and, under `npm run dev` with `?area=dev-test-area`, swimming a tall area that scrolls up and down (H1, X10). Under `npm run dev` with `?decor`, they also cover placing, moving, and putting away the Floating and Hanging test Decorations, and the Home Tank controls, at 460 x 320, 320 x 440, and 1280 x 720 (C7, C9, open question 145). For the lily pad pond (issue #75), they cover swimming Area 2 to its exit, whether it feels a little harder than the canal, the plastic worms (the stiff, shiny tell against the wiggling real worms, in colour and in greyscale; the "bleh", the green cheeks and the wobble never looking scary or sick; the tint without the wobble under reduced motion; and how the queasy slowdown feels), the tray of 8 Decorations, and the pond's boot bake size, measured by the owner (open question 127). For the pond at dusk (Area 3, issue #98), they cover swimming Area 3 to its exit, whether it feels a little harder than Area 2; the two wading herons (issue #111): that they stand in the water with their heads above it and wade slowly, and that a heron's head follows the axolotl when it is near (open question 218); whether the S-neck pose reads as a warning without a "!" bubble, in colour, in greyscale, and under reduced motion, where only the pose changes (open question 212); that swimming away during the S dodges the stab, that staying costs one leg with the usual poof and protected time, and that the heron never grabs, holds, or lifts the axolotl; that the bottom band and hiding in reeds, under a lily pad, or in the root arch bring no warning and no stab (open questions 207 and 208); that only one heron or tilapia warns at a time; that Area 3 can be beaten with one leg and every Epic and Legendary spot reached with timing; whether "stay deep, or hide" is easy to learn; that it never looks scary; and the frame time in Area 3 with both herons (X11); the darting snacks (whether the hop feels playful, not unfair); the dusk look and the fireflies, still under reduced motion; the stream mouth beside the root arch (open question 172); Firefly gold against the golden colours that are not Dream colours, by eye (open question 175); the Water-lily shirt's look (open question 177); and the tray of 14 Decorations, the wide Firefly string lights included. For the refuge (N2, issue #74), they cover the Refuge tile and its "Refuge grew" badge, in colour and in greyscale; the clean-up after beating an area, and its fade under reduced motion (open question 185); the milestone card after beating the last area of a habitat, after "Try it now!" too; panning by drag, wheel, Previous and Next, and keys; the refuge art; and the hub at 460 x 320 and 480 x 375 with Refuge reaching over the floor Decorations (open question 178).
