The area table puts Area 3 in the lily pad pond habitat (N2), so its dusk look is a variant, not a new habitat. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 169 | Are the heron's reach and its strike zone on the risk ladder the same rule (B10, N3)? | Superseded by #111: no longer exactly one rule. A heron warns from where it stands, within 110 pixels, so its strike zone, every point within S = 110 of its stretch, holds every point it can warn at from somewhere on its stretch, but at any moment it reaches only the points within 110 pixels of where it stands. Cover stops a warning but does not change the risk ladder (open questions 207 and 215). Before #111: Built: yes, one measure. A point is in the heron's reach exactly when its route distance to the heron's stretch is at most 60 pixels and it is outside the safe area, and the risk ladder uses the same distance, with S = 60. So a spot the ladder calls Epic or Legendary is always one the heron can strike, and a Common or Rare spot never is. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 170 | Does the heron ever bump the axolotl (A4, N3)? | Built: no. The heron species is marked as never touching, so it never bumps, whatever its touch reach. Only its stab can reach the axolotl, as one hit (issue #111; open question 211). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 171 | Is it fine that the decorate caption hides with all 14 Decorations in the tray at the smallest canvases (C9)? | Built: yes, by the caption's existing rule: it shows only where it has room under the controls. With all 14 in the tray, it has no room at 460 x 320 or 320 x 440, so it hides there; at 340 x 440 it shows. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 172 | Where does the stream art start when the current is not at the area's end (N2)? | Built: the stream mouth is at the right edge of the screen column that holds the current's right end, so the art sits just upstream of the current. In Area 3 that edge is in open water, beside the root arch, so the band and its flow marks fade out over the last 40 pixels before the mouth, and the mouth stones that spill past the edge are drawn on the next screen. The Area 3 stream reads as flowing from under the bank roots. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 173 | May a trip fact card's body wrap wider when it does not fit (C14)? | Built: yes. The body first wraps at the body width; only when that does not fit the canvas height does it wrap at the title's tight-padding width, which takes fewer lines on canvases under 484 pixels wide. Today only the heron card at 320 x 440 uses it: 9 lines at 240 pixels, but 8 at 264, using 397 of 408 pixels of height. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 174 | How many treasures can one tank arrival or banking card name (A12, B9)? | Built: the card fit test bounds one trip by `MAX_TRIP_FINDS`, the largest area catalogue, 24 (the canal's), because a trip finds only its own area's treasures. Wording that names up to the whole catalogue, 60 treasures with Area 4's (issue #76) and 72 with Area 5's (issue #103), is still checked for clipping on every supported canvas. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 175 | What colour is Firefly gold (B3, F15)? | Built: a solid, Rare Dream colour with the body colour #ffd52a, shown with its name label like the other solid colours. It is the 14th Dream colour, held by Area 3's one Paint shell. It is told apart from every other Dream colour in greyscale and in colour, but it is close to golds that are not Dream colours, the Golden albino morph's #ffd36b and the Legendary badge gold, so the owner is to check it by eye ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md)). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 176 | How does the heron's shadow appear and leave (N3, X3)? | Superseded by #111: the shadow is gone. The herons stand in the water and are always shown, and under reduced motion each pose change is a switch with no tween (open question 212). Before #111: Built: under full motion it fades in over the first 0.4 seconds of its glide-in and out over the last 0.4 seconds of its glide-off, so it never pops in or out at the ends of its route; it darkens for the warning with a 0.4 second crossfade. Under reduced motion it fades in where it is over the last 0.4 seconds of the glide-in and out over 0.4 seconds where it was, and darkens at once. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 177 | What does the Water-lily shirt look like (B3, F13)? | Built: a pale water-blue shirt on the Tank top's silhouette, with one hem band across the torso and a small lily blossom on the chest. The Rare step of the Wearable look ladder allows one trim band; the blossom is a second detail, so whether a Rare shirt may carry both is the owner's ruling ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md)). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 178 | Where does the best-stars badge go when the hub reaches over the floor Decorations (C7)? | Decided: option A. When the hub reaches, the badge always uses its stacked layout, the area label above the stars (about 104 x 78.5, with the same 18 pixel text and 24 pixel stars), in the bottom-right corner, never centred. Centred, it does not fit at 480 x 375 and 480 x 414. The focus caption stays 17 pixels clear of it at 460 wide and 27 pixels at 480 wide (owner ruling on issue #74, 2026-10-22). |
| 179 | What is the habitat milestone card's title (N2)? | Built, provisional: "All clean!". It hides where it does not fit, at 460 to 1024 wide by 320, 340, and 360 tall; the picture, the body, and "Keep exploring" always show. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 180 | When does a habitat milestone card show (N2, C15)? | Built, provisional: on the Home Tank return after the trip, after the tank arrival card and its first-find cards, and before the hub takes input, one card per newly clean habitat, in habitat order. After the Paint shell card's "Try it now!", the milestone cards show first, then restyle opens. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 181 | Which axolotl does "See it again" name (N2)? | Built, provisional: the current explorer, because the save does not keep which axolotl cleaned up each habitat. The card shown after the trip names the trip's explorer. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 182 | What are the refuge fact cards' unfound hints (C14)? | Built, provisional: "Visit the refuge to find this card." for "Axolotl refuges", and "Beat an area, then visit the refuge to find this card." for "Canal clean-ups". The habitat hint that the area cards use does not fit, because neither card belongs to an area. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 183 | Do the refuge fact cards have a picture in the Collection Book (C14)? | Built, provisional: no. Neither has Book art, so each found card shows its text only, like the plastic worm and heron cards. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 184 | Where do the refuge's Previous and Next tiles go (N2)? | Built, provisional: labelled tiles, an arrow over the label, Previous 118 x 64 and Next 65 x 64, at the top right, beside Back, when the canvas is at least 439 pixels wide (room for Back, Previous and Next 12 pixels apart inside the margins); else in a centred bottom row. At 460 x 320 they sit at the top and two whole sections still show; at 320 x 440 they sit in the bottom row. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 185 | How long does the refuge clean-up take (N2, X3)? | Built, provisional: a 0.6 second hold after the refuge opens; the cleared trash floats up 24 pixels and fades with a sparkle over 0.9 seconds; then a newly clean section crossfades to its clean picture as its plants grow, over 0.9 seconds. A pan glides for 0.3 seconds. Under reduced motion each step is a 0.2 second fade, with no float, sparkle, or growth, and a pan is at once. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 186 | Is it fine that five hub actions make the hub compact on more canvases (C7)? | Built, provisional: yes, by the existing rule that full size shows only where it fits. The hub is now compact at 320, 340, 459, 460, and 520 x 568, 320, 340, and 520 x 600, 480 x 568, and 844 x 320 to 480. The "Home Tank" name panel now shows at 320, 340, 459, 460, and 520 x 568 and 320, 340, and 520 x 600, and now hides at 320, 340, and 520 x 667, 459, 460, and 480 x 600, and 844 x 568, 600, 667, and 844. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 187 | Are the refuge fact cards true (C13, C14)? | Built, provisional: the bodies and sources in C14. The owner is to read the sources and check both cards before release. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 188 | What does an unfound treasure's area hint say (C11)? | Built, provisional: the area label and the habitat name, as in "Area N, Habitat: ", and then the rarity hint with its first letter lower-cased, such as "Area 2, Lily pad pond: look off the main path!". The rarity hints keep their text, so the Epic hint keeps "or log": "Area 3, Lily pad pond: look in a cave or log, or past the current!". Issue #106's example, "Area 2, Lily pad pond: look in a cave or past the current!", leaves "or log" out. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 189 | Which area does a Dream colour's hint name, and a treasure in more than one area (C11)? | Built, provisional: a Dream colour names the area of its Paint shell. A treasure names the first area, in area-table order, whose layout holds it, found from the area data and never from its id. Today every treasure and every Paint shell is in exactly one area. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 190 | Where does the "Treasure Chest" title hide (C11)? | Built, provisional: only where even the short section lines leave no room for one whole row, as the Collection Book's title does (C14). The heading moves up to the title's place and the list gains 47 pixels. Measured, it hides only at 320 x 440, 340 x 440, 360 x 440, and 460 x 320. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 191 | Are the Area 4 layout and tuning values right (N2, H1, H4)? | Built, provisional (issue #76): 6 x 2 screens; the Home Spot at (120, 200), with its safe area its column, x 40 to 300, from the surface to the bed; the exit at (2780, 625), radius 40; 2 tilapia; one current, x 2140 to 2340 and y 380 to 600, flowing left at 72 pixels per second, so one leg makes 18 pixels per second of headway; 28 snacks (16 worms and 12 bugs, 4 in the current), drifting with periods of 7.6 and 6.08 seconds, about 15.6 pixels per second at most; 3 darters by the shared dart rule; 9 treasure slots (they often stay empty under the one-step fallback; open question 220); 6 stranding places; and 5 ghost nets. The owner set the size, the counts, and the slots; the rest are derived. To tune after play-testing. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 192 | Are Area 4's tilapia values right, and how are they carried (N2, N3, B10)? | Built, provisional: the owner's values (a 0.85 second warning, strike speed 400, reach 145, a 2.2 second cooldown, and patrol speed 50), with the 0.8 second floor, the 0.4 second strike, and every other value kept. The tuning is optional area layout data; an area without it plays and bands exactly as before. With it, the area's tilapia routes are banded by its own strike range, S = 145 (Common over 205, Rare over 145, Epic over 72.5, Legendary within 72.5), and its fairness check plays the same tuning. Whether the step feels a little quicker but still fair is a play-test check. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 193 | Is the ghost net's snag right (N3)? | Built, provisional: 50% top speed for 2 seconds, multiplied with the leg factor, with the acceleration and Tail Dash unchanged; a snag only on entering a net's reach, never each step inside, never stacked, and one entry for overlapping nets; a meet range of 100 pixels from the net's edge, like the plastic worm's; the message "Snagged on an old net! Wriggle free." in the snack banner's place; a wriggle of 0.1 radians, one cycle every 0.4 seconds, under full motion only; and a small snag cue over the head in both motion modes. The owner set the slowdown, the time, no stacking, and the reduced-motion rule; the rest are derived. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 194 | How near must the axolotl come to snag on a net (N3)? | Built, provisional: 18 pixels from the net's rectangle edge to the body centre, the edge included. It is the half height of a Medium Regular body with its ink outline, (19 + 3.5) x 0.95 x 0.85, about 18.2, rounded, so a snag starts when the inked top or bottom of a Medium body reaches the net, and a head or tail swimming in end-on pokes into the loose mesh first. It is the same at every size and age, because size is for looks only (F4), as the tilapia's reach matches Medium. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 195 | How far from a current must a ghost net be (A9, N3)? | Built, provisional: at least 190.5 pixels. That is the farthest a snagged axolotl at the speed perk cap swims before its snag ends, 2 seconds x 150 x 1.15 x 0.5 = 172.5 pixels, plus the 18 pixel net reach, worked out the way the plastic worm's 371.5 pixels was. A snagged one-leg axolotl swims 45 pixels per second, under the 72 pixel per second current, so a snag inside the current could not be beaten; this clearance means it never happens. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 196 | How is the level layout ladder read without walls (N2)? | Built, provisional: the obstacles are the ghost nets (kept out of by their reach) and the tilapia routes (by their strike range); the branching routes are the top-row and bottom-row ways; the dead ends are the root tangle in the bottom left and the bank in the top right, both holding treasure; a gap is the clear width between two obstacles' keep-outs, along the shortest line between them, measured in a test (`src/logic/chinampaLayout.test.ts`). The safe passing width is one body height, twice the net reach, 36 pixels, so the axolotl may stray half a body either way off the middle of a gap and still pass clear. Area 4's narrowest main-path gap is 64 pixels, against about 136.09 in Area 3 (between its first two plastic worms; issue #111 raised the heron's reach to 110 pixels, so the heron no longer bounds Area 3's narrowest gap), and no gap beside a net is under 36. If a later layout needs solid terrain, that is a framework change, in its own PR with an ADR (N2, the area shape rule). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 197 | How are Area 4's under-bank spots made (B5)? | Built, provisional: a new cover, under the bank, from new optional area data, one overhang rectangle under each top-row garden plot bank, from the surface down to y 140 and inset 20 pixels from its screen's edges; there is none over the gap current's column. It is never built from the bank art's columns, so the canal's and the pond's covers do not change. It is shallow cover, for Common and Rare spots only. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 198 | How does the decorate tray fit 20 Decorations (C9)? | Built, provisional: tray paging (owner decision on issue #76, 2026-10-23, <https://github.com/brenpike/glam-gills/issues/76#issuecomment-6355162967>). Under the tray rule alone, the tray fit every supported canvas only up to 17 Decorations. Now a tray that fits neither the usual rooms nor the reach rooms goes in pages of at most two rows of squares (one where two do not fit), as many a row as fit across, with Back and Next on the row of Put away and Done, wrapping past either end. Back and Next are the Treasure Chest's 78 x 64 tiles, or 48 x 48 icon-only tiles named by the caption when compact. They show only with more than one page, and a tray that fits on one page keeps its layout exactly. Every target stays at least 48 x 48, nothing goes into the top band, and it keeps working as more Decorations ship. With 20 shipped, the tray pages only for 18 to 20 at 360 x 440 and 375 x 440, and for 19 or 20 at 460 and 480 wide by 320, 340, 360, and 375 tall and at 480 x 414. At 460 x 320: 14 a page (two rows of 7), so 20 take pages of 14 and 6; Back {85, 236, 48, 48}, Next {145, 236, 48, 48}, Put away {205, 236, 48, 48}, Done {265, 228, 110, 64}; no caption. At 360 x 440: 10 a page (two rows of 5 on the wall, y 16 and 76); Back {35, 271, 48, 48}, Next {95, 271, 48, 48}, Put away {155, 271, 48, 48}, Done {215, 263, 110, 64} in the open water; the caption at y 339. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 199 | Is the ghost net fact card true (C13)? | Built, provisional: the owner's title and body, word for word, with the two NOAA Marine Debris Program sources in C13. The sources support lost nets trapping animals and people removing them from lakes and waterways, but neither names canals. The owner is to read the sources and check the word "canals" before release. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 200 | How wide are the Papel picado banner and the Willow swing (B3, C9)? | Built, provisional: the Papel picado banner is 144 tank units wide, like the Firefly string lights, so its base x runs from 96 to 384 (open question 142); the Willow swing has the standard width, 80. Both are Hanging. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 201 | Which perk does each Area 4 Wearable give (B3, F9)? | Built, provisional: one existing perk each, at the canal's value for its rarity: Straw sun hat Treasure sense 15%, Marigold necklace Regrowth 10%, Garden overalls Snack magnet 40, Monarch hair clip Dash 20%, and Monarch wings Speed 10%. None is a Shell perk. No Wearable has a set ID yet. The owner asked to check these in the PR. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 202 | What colour is Poppy red (B3, F15)? | Built, provisional: a solid, Rare Dream colour with the body colour #e8402f, shown with its name label like the other solid colours. It is the 15th Dream colour, held by Area 4's one Paint shell. Its body is 3.57:1 with ink and 4.03:1 with white. It is told apart from every other Dream colour in greyscale and in colour; its closest is Rainbow, which also carries its bands ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md)). The owner is to check it by eye. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 203 | Where are the chinampa edges of the gap current drawn (N2)? | Built, provisional: one chinampa edge just above the current's rectangle and one just below it, 2 pixels off it, centred on it and flared past its ends, with their roots streaming the way the water flows. They are placed from the current's rectangle only, so they are art, not obstacles. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 204 | Where does the Home Spot's burrow stand when the Home Spot is in the top row of a tall area (A13, N2)? | Built, provisional: in a one-row area, and in the bottom row of a tall one, the burrow stands on the bed as before. A tall area's top row has no bed, so there the burrow stands at the Home Spot itself, its arch centred on it and ringed by the safe-ring dots, so the burrow and its flag show where the explorer banks. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 205 | Is the boot bake memory still fine with Area 4 (open question 127)? | Built, provisional: every area's scenery is still baked when the game starts. By estimate, a 6 x 2 area bakes about twice Area 3's 15.8 MiB of scenery at bake scale 2, plus the chinampa props. Baking each area only when it is played is still a follow-up ([ADR 0018](../adr/0018-chinampa-gardens-ghost-nets-and-predator-ladder.md)). The owner is to measure boot memory and frame time in Area 4 on the phone. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 206 | Does the chinampa look read (N2)? | Built, provisional: the look, the root tangle, the gap edges, the ghost net mesh, and the area map's garden plot marker in the [art style guide](../art-style-guide.md). The owner is to check them by eye, in colour and in greyscale, and that the garden plots read as distinct from the canal's willow banks. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 207 | What is cover from a heron, and what does it change (B10, N3)? | Decided (owner decision on issue #111, 2026-10-23): "The bottom band and hiding spots are safe" means a heron hunts by sight and cannot see an axolotl in cover: inside a reed patch, under a lily pad, or in the root arch. Past the current is open water, not cover. A heron does not warn at an axolotl in cover, a warning is cancelled if its target goes into cover, and a stab never connects with an axolotl in cover. The tilapia ignores cover, as before. Treasure spots may still sit within a heron's reach (B10). Built: a reed patch is cover over its whole rectangle, from the surface to the sea bed top, not only where reeds are drawn; under a lily pad is within the pad's half-width and from the surface down to 30 pixels below it, for every lily pad, the pads for scenery included; and the root arch is its whole cave box. Cover does not stop a meeting (open question 216). The risk ladder still measures distance only, so a spot in cover still counts as near a heron, because getting in and out of it crosses the heron's reach (open question 215). This replaces "No cover shields the axolotl: only depth does" (issue #98). The other reading, that no treasure spot may sit in a heron's reach, is not used. Needs owner confirmation of the built details. |
| 208 | How far does a heron reach (N3)? | Built, provisional: a heron's stretch is the line where its beak rests, at y 120. It reaches 110 pixels from where it stands on that line, so the deepest point it can warn at is y 230, the owner's "about y 230", and the bottom band below that, down to the sea bed top at y 318, gets no warning. A stab hits within 26 pixels of its tip, so the lowest axolotl centre a stab can hit is about y 256. The art draws each heron standing with its head above the water and its beak tip resting near the water line over its point, and draws every stab from the drawn beak to the spot the rules use ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) 7h). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 209 | When does a heron warn (A1, A2, N3)? | Built, provisional: on sight, like the tilapia: when the axolotl is within 110 pixels of where the heron stands, outside the safe area, not in cover, and no other predator is warning or striking (A1). The warning lasts its 1.2 second wind-up, above the 0.8 second floor (A2), which does not change. The heron stands still while it warns; only its facing follows the axolotl. This replaces the timer-started warning of issue #98. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 210 | Where does a heron's stab go, and what does the end-of-warning check do (N3)? | Built, provisional: to the spot where the axolotl was when the warning started. This is a shared rule option, the fixed strike spot, used by the heron and not by the tilapia, which still locks its aim at the strike start, so the tilapia's play does not change. The end-of-warning check is the shared one: if the axolotl is out of reach, in the safe area, or in cover, or the species' rule says no, the warning is cancelled and the 3 second cooldown runs. If the axolotl has only moved, the stab still goes to the fixed spot and misses. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 211 | How does a heron's stab move and hit (A4, N3)? | Built, provisional: the heron stays where it stands through the warning, the stab, and the cooldown. The stab's tip moves in a straight line from the heron to the fixed spot over 0.3 seconds. It connects when the tip is within 26 pixels of the axolotl, the tilapia's Medium hit reach, outside the safe area and not in cover, checked on every capped game step. The heron's strike speed is its reach over its stab time, about 367 pixels per second, recorded as the stab's top speed; no heron rule reads it. A stab that connects is one hit (A4, A5, A11, A22), with the usual bump cooldown, never a bump. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 212 | What is a heron's warning cue (A2, X2, X3, N3)? | Built, provisional: the S-neck pose. The heron freezes and draws its neck back into an S, a clearly different silhouette, which carries the cue in greyscale. There is no "!" bubble and no glint; A2 allows a pose change, and open question 160 was the precedent. The stab is the neck straight toward the spot, with the beak closed. Under reduced motion every change is a pose switch with no tween, and the heron still wades, but its legs do not step ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) 7h). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 213 | What does a heron do between strikes (N3)? | Built, provisional: after a stab, hit or miss, or a cancelled warning, it stands still for its 3 second cooldown, with its beak back at rest and its facing following the axolotl, then wades on. Standing still through the cooldown is a shared rule option the tilapia does not use. It wades back and forth along a two-point stretch 180 pixels long, at 20 pixels per second (the tilapia patrols at 45), so a wade there and back takes 18 seconds. These values are provisional. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 214 | Where do the two herons stand in Area 3 (N2, B10)? | Built, provisional: both at the far end, at y 120: `heron-0` from x 2190 to 2370, over the spots past the stream, and `heron-1` from x 2440 to 2620, over the root arch. `heron-0` and `heron-1` are stable route ids ([ADR 0019](../adr/0019-heron-wading-stalker.md)). Every Epic and Legendary spot there stays near a heron (B10), the deep lane stays open, and no tilapia route moves. To keep every spot 60 pixels from every heron waypoint and in its band, the spots past the stream and in the root arch are re-laid with the same ids: past the stream, Legendary pots at (2252, 120) and (2308, 120) and Epic spots at (2260, 190) and (2330, 196); in the root arch, Legendary pots at (2500, 112) and (2556, 112) and Epic spots at (2470, 196) and (2540, 196). The root arch has 4 spots, down from 6: `pond-2/spot-cave-3` is now Epic (it was Legendary), and `pond-2/spot-cave-5` and `pond-2/spot-cave-6` are gone. The main swim path still runs at y 200, so under the stretches it is inside the herons' reach. The stranding places, plastic worms, pads, reed columns, tilapia routes, exit, and cave box do not change. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 215 | What is a heron's strike range on the risk ladder (B10)? | Built, provisional: S = 110, its reach, through the shared bands, measured as route distance from its stretch, as for the tilapia: Common farther than 170 pixels, Rare more than 110 and at most 170, Epic more than 55 and at most 110, and Legendary at most 55. A heron warns from where it stands, so its strike zone holds every point it can warn at from somewhere on its stretch, rather than being exactly the same rule; this replaces open question 169. The ladder measures distance only, so a spot in cover still counts as near a heron (open question 207). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 216 | When is a heron met (C14, N3)? | Built, provisional: with no gap, the herons are always shown. This replaces open question 158. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 217 | What happens to the timer-started warning and the heron's shadow cycle (N2, N3, Implementation Decision 8)? | Built, provisional: both are removed, with the heron's cycle tuning, because no species uses a timer any more (#108 drops Area 10's 6 second cycle too). | 218 | When does a heron's head follow the axolotl (N3)? | Built, provisional: only while the axolotl is within 180 pixels of the heron, its meet range, and not in cover. | 219 | How does the Area 3 fairness check stay non-empty for a pot in cover (B10)? | Built, provisional: the check that an axolotl waiting at each Legendary pot is hit waits at the pot itself when the pot is in open water. | 220 | Do Area 4's treasure slots often stay empty under the one-step fallback (B4, N2)? | Open (issues #111 and #76): in a measured 200 fresh trips, about 136 left 1 or 2 of Area 4's 9 slots empty (84 left one, 52 left two). Area 4 has 4 Common and 4 Rare treasures, and the Scarecrow, a Rare, is always placed as the special treasure, so only 7 Common and Rare treasures are left to fill the slots; a slot that rolled Common or Rare with none left takes only the one-step fallback of B4, and stays empty if that finds nothing. The owner checks in play whether that feels fine, or whether Area 4 needs more Common and Rare treasures or fewer slots. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 221 | What are Area 5's stable ids (N2, B3, D2)? | Built, provisional (issue #103, named default ND-1; [ADR 0020](../adr/0020-u-turn-areas-garter-snake-and-hyacinth-mats.md)): the area `chinampa-2`, in `src/logic/chinampa2Layout.ts`, with `CHINAMPA_2_AREA`, `CHINAMPA_2_LAYOUT`, and `CHINAMPA_2_TILAPIA`; the treasures, in `CHINAMPA_2_TREASURES` after `CHINAMPA_TREASURES`, `chinampa/watering-can`, `chinampa/floating-gourd`, `chinampa/bee-headband`, `chinampa/bandana`, `chinampa/pinata` (named "Piñata"), `chinampa/sunflower-sunglasses`, `chinampa/embroidered-shirt`, `chinampa/paint-shell-plum`, `chinampa/talavera-fountain`, `chinampa/hummingbird-tail-ribbon`, `chinampa/alebrije-statue`, and `chinampa/rainbow-serape-cape`, keeping the habitat prefix as Area 3 kept `pond/`; the Dream colour `plum`; the predator species and fact card `garter-snake`, on routes `garter-snake-0` and `garter-snake-1`; the element type and fact card `water-hyacinth`, with spawns `chinampa-2/hyacinth-mats-1` and `chinampa-2/hyacinth-mats-2`; the nets `chinampa-2/ghost-net-1` to `chinampa-2/ghost-net-5`; and the spots, the cave, and the stranding places `chinampa-2/...`. | 222 | Where are Area 5's exit and safe area (N2, H2)? | Built, provisional (issue #103, named default ND-2): the exit at (120, 625), radius 40, straight under the Home Spot; the safe area the Home Spot's column in the top row only, x 40 to 300, from the surface down to y 360, so the exit is outside it, as every area's must be. | 223 | Which side of a current is past it (B6)? | Built, provisional (issue #103, named default ND-3): the side its flow comes from. A spot is past a current when it is within 140 pixels beyond the current's upstream edge and inside the current's y range; a current with no sideways flow has no past side, as [ADR 0018](../adr/0018-chinampa-gardens-ghost-nets-and-predator-ladder.md) says. One shared pure function decides the side, for both the hiding rules and the past-current marker. In Areas 1 to 4 every current flows left with home to its left, so the side is the same as before; a test shows it for each of them, and the goldens prove it too. Area 5's current flows right, against the main path where the path crosses it, so its past side is x 2320 to 2460, the far side along the path. The other option was the side where the main path leaves the current. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 224 | Which way does the open exit sign's arrow point (H2)? | Built, provisional (issue #103, named default ND-4): toward the nearer side edge of the area: left when the exit's centre is in the left half of the area, else right. | 225 | What makes an area a U-turn (N2, the area shape rule)? | Built, provisional (issue #103, named default ND-5): the Home Spot is in the top-left screen; the exit is in the bottom-left screen, in the Home Spot's screen column; the main path starts at the start and ends at the exit; along the top row x never decreases, the path goes down in the rightmost screen column, and along the bottom row x never increases. Area 5's shape test checks exactly this. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 226 | How do the water hyacinth mats look and gate the way in the side view (N2, N3)? | Built, provisional, the key default for the owner to confirm (issue #103, named default ND-6): Area 4 draws the gap between two chinampas with its edges above and below the current, and the channel between two mats uses the same mapping. Each pair is one mat above and one mat below a narrow horizontal channel, and the two drift together along y to close it. A pair spans its row's full height at its x, so a closed pair gates that row, and the way round is through the other row. Pair 1 gates the top row at x 1500 to 1650, its channel at y 220; pair 2 gates the bottom row at x 580 to 730, its channel at y 500. The way round pair 1, through the bottom row, passes `chinampa-2/ghost-net-4` under it, and the way round pair 2, through the top row, passes `chinampa-2/ghost-net-1` over it, so waiting for the channel is the skilful choice and going round the risky one. The two pairs never gate both rows at the same x, and a way to the exit is always open. The other reading: both mats float at the surface and drift together along x. If the owner rejects this reading, the build stops and asks on #103. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 227 | How do the mats time their cycle (N3)? | Built, provisional (issue #103, named default ND-7): an 8 second cycle, open for 3 seconds, the first 1 second of which the mats drift apart, mirroring the 1 second of closing, then closing for 1 second (the warning), then closed for 4 seconds. Pair 1 starts its cycle at 0 seconds and pair 2 at 4 seconds, so when one is open the other is closed. The channel counts as open once its clear height is at least 36 pixels, the safe passing width; fully open, it is 72 pixels tall, two safe passing widths. A channel is short enough that a one-leg axolotl that enters as it opens is through before closing starts: at most 189 pixels long, 150 x 0.6 x (3 − 0.5) − 2 x 18, and a longer one is refused; Area 5's channels are 150 pixels. The tuning is `HYACINTH_MATS` in `src/logic/tuning.ts`. The element type keeps its kind, a cycle of period 8, and gains only optional fields, so the timing rule and existing data do not change. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 228 | How do the mats block, and what must stay clear of them (N3)? | Built, provisional (issue #103, named default ND-8): each mat is solid at all times, at its position for the current time, and the explorer's body centre stays out of each mat grown by 18 pixels, the net reach, the body's half height. A mat that moves onto the explorer pushes it out along the channel to the channel's nearer end, or to the other end when the nearer one is in a net's reach, a current, another mat, or out of the swim bounds. Tail Dash and a bump do not pass through a mat. Only the explorer is blocked: the following rescued axolotl, the predators, the snacks, and the carry home are not. So every predator route and its reach, every snack start, every darted snack landing (an optional mats list in the dart rule, absent in Areas 3 and 4), every hiding spot, stranding place, and net, and the exit stay clear of each mat pair's whole sweep, and nothing a player needs is ever inside a mat. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 229 | When are the mats met, and how do element fact cards work (C14, N3)? | Built, provisional (issue #103, named default ND-9): the mats are met within 180 pixels of a pair's sweep, outside the safe area. The Collection Book lists an area's element cards after its threat cards, in area-table order, so the 9 cards are the tilapia, the plastic worm, the heron, the ghost net, the garter snake, the water hyacinth, "Axolotl refuges", "Canal clean-ups", and "Boy or girl?". Each unfound card's hint names the habitat of the first area that has it, so both new cards say "Explore the chinampa gardens to find this card." Counting new things per area keeps threats and elements apart, so Area 5 adds one of each. The new ids join the hazards met under ADR 0012's additive rule, with no save change. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 230 | Where do the garter snakes swim, and how deep do they reach (N3)? | Built, provisional (issue #103, named default ND-10, with the owner's option 1 in open question 247): each stretch is straight, at y 100, the snake's head line, with two waypoints, 200 pixels long (`garter-snake-0` from x 1040 to 1240, `garter-snake-1` from x 2600 to 2800), in open top-row water under no bank. With the warn on route rule, it warns at no axolotl deeper than y 212, hits none deeper than y 254, and its head goes no deeper than y 228, so the bottom row is out of its reach, and under each stretch a deep lane below y 260 is out of every snake's reach and more than 150 pixels from every tilapia route. | 247 | How is a garter snake kept from warning deeper and deeper after each dart (N3)? | Decided (owner decision on issue #103, 2026-10-23, option 1, <https://github.com/brenpike/glam-gills/issues/103#issuecomment-6371850978>): a new shared rule option, warn on route (`warnOnRoute`). Under the plain shared rules a snake could warn again from wherever a dart left it, and a probe walked a snake down to y 599. With the option, a predator warns only while it is within 2 pixels of its route as a closed loop, inclusive; off its route it does not warn and cancels a warning at its end, and it swims its route through the cooldown and patrol, warning again once back on it. The garter snake opts in; the tilapia and the heron do not, so they play as before. The dart itself is unchanged and has no depth cap. The bounds: warnings at y 212 or less, hits at y 254 or less, the head at y 228 or less, and y 260 and below safe. Measured on Area 5's real routes over 60 seconds: the deepest head at y 227.96, the deepest warning target at y 206.96, and no events in the deep lane. Options 2 (a dart depth cap) and 3 (both) were not chosen. |
| 248 | What are the danger pass's stable ids and names (N2, N3, H4)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-1; [ADR 0021](../adr/0021-danger-pass-chase-rule-and-moving-hazards.md)). Named defaults ND-1 to ND-31 were posted on issue #117 before building as provisional build choices the owner can override (<https://github.com/brenpike/glam-gills/issues/117#issuecomment-6377797982>; no owner reply yet). The chase: `ChaseTuning` {sight, leash, speed} and an optional `chase` on the predator tuning in `src/logic/tuning.ts`, the defaults `TILAPIA_CHASE`, `TILAPIA_RAMP_CHASE`, `GARTER_SNAKE_CHASE`, and `CARP_CHASE`, and `tunedSpecies(species, tuning)` in `src/logic/predators.ts`. The carp: species and fact card id `carp`, named "Common carp"; `CARP_SPECIES`, after the garter snake in the species list; `CARP` in `tuning.ts`; `CARP_FACT` and `CARP_MEET_RANGE` in `src/logic/factCards.ts`; and phase 2 route ids `carp-<n>`. Moving hazards: `src/logic/movingHazards.ts`, with `MovingHazardType`, `MovingHazardSpawn`, `movingHazardSpawn()`, and `PUNTING_POLE`, whose type, look, and fact card id is `punting-pole`, named "Punting pole"; its tuning `PUNTING_POLES` (`PuntingPoleTuning`) in `tuning.ts`; the area data field `movingHazards`; and spawn ids `<area id>/punting-pole-<n>`. Drifting nets: an optional `drift` (`NetDrift`: dx, dy, period, offset) on a hazard spawn, with `hazardShapeAt` and `hazardSweep` in `src/logic/hazards.ts`, `netDrift()` in `src/logic/ghostNets.ts`, and `DRIFTING_NETS` (`DriftingNetTuning`) in `tuning.ts`. The danger part mark: `dangerPart: true` on a species (the carp), and on every moving hazard type. The dev area: `dev-danger-area`, Area 98, `DEV_DANGER_AREA`. New modules: `src/logic/dangerBar.ts` and `src/logic/riskFairness.ts`. Built on top: `factCards.ts` became a runtime leaf module to fix an import cycle, with `src/logic/moduleCycles.test.ts` as its regression test. Earlier ids and order never change. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 249 | How does the chase rule work (N3, A1, A3)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-2): only a predator whose tuning has a chase chases, and the tilapia, heron, garter snake, and carp tunings have none, so without a chase every predator plays step for step as before. It chases only on patrol; if it may warn (A1, A3), it warns as before. Otherwise, when the axolotl is exposed (outside the safe area and, for a sight hunter, not in cover) and within its sight, it swims toward it at its chase speed, stopping just inside its lunge range (1e-6 pixels inside, so it can warn), where it warns, or waits in place while another predator warns or strikes (A1). It never ends a step more than its leash from its patrol loop; a step that would go farther ends on the leash's edge, on the line from the nearest point of the loop. It chases only while it is within its leash of its loop; beyond it (for example after a strike) it swims back along its route. The bounds and the safe area keep-out apply to every move; where they conflict, it holds its place. It faces the way it moves. Out of sight or with the axolotl in the safe area, it swims its route at patrol speed. The warning, the strike, and the cooldown do not change. A warn on route species chases along its route only, with its leash counted as 0, so the garter snake slides along its stretch and its depth bounds (open question 247) hold. Chasing is not attacking, so any number of predators may chase at once. There is no new cue, and reduced motion does not change the chase. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 250 | What are the chase limits (N3)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-3): the speed strictly between the 2-leg top speed (112.5 pixels per second) and the 4-leg top speed (150), worked out from the swim top speed and the leg factors; the sight greater than the lunge range of the tuning that carries the chase; and the leash 0 or more. `tunedSpecies` refuses anything else, and any value that is not a finite number, with a RangeError, and refuses a chase on a species with the fixed strike spot or still cooldown option (the heron). A test checks every default against the limits. A snag (half speed), queasy time (60%), or lost legs can leave the axolotl slower than a chaser; Tail Dash (330 pixels per second) always outruns one. An area's own tilapia tuning is not checked against these limits when it is built, so each phase 2 issue that adds a tilapia chase checks it in its own layout test (a residual in [ADR 0021](../adr/0021-danger-pass-chase-rule-and-moving-hazards.md)). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 251 | What chase does each predator type get (N3)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-4), in no shipped area yet; phase 2 uses them: a tilapia in a full-bar area (Area 4 on), `TILAPIA_CHASE`, sight 240 pixels, leash 160, speed 125 pixels per second; a tilapia in a ramp-bar area (Areas 2 and 3), `TILAPIA_RAMP_CHASE`, sight 200, leash 80, speed 115, with Area 2's leash shorter than Area 3's, for example 60 (issue #121); the garter snake, `GARTER_SNAKE_CHASE`, sight 200, leash 0 (along its stretch), speed 120; and the carp, `CARP_CHASE`, sight 220, leash 120, speed 115. The heron has none and keeps its wading stalk (issue #120). The owner is to check in play that a 4-leg axolotl can swim away and a 1-leg one can escape with Tail Dash. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 252 | Does the risk ladder count a chaser's leash (B10, N2)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-5), a key default for the owner to decide: no. The risk ladder (B10) and the gap measure ignore the chase: a chasing predator's strike range S stays its lunge range, measured from its patrol loop, and the gap measure keeps out of it by the same reach. With the leash added, a Common spot in a full-bar area would need to be about 365 pixels from every tilapia loop, which leaves almost no Common spots; and the chase widens where a predator can warn by the same leash for every spot near it, so the ladder's order still holds. Only the danger bar counts the leash (open question 259). The other option: S = lunge range + leash. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 253 | What is the common carp (N3)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-6), in no shipped area yet: a predator on the shared rules, with no rule options, a danger part. Its tuning: a 0.8 second floor, a 1.0 second warning, a 120 pixel lunge range, a sweep at 200 pixels per second for 0.6 seconds (120 pixels), a 38 pixel hit reach (the tilapia's is 26), a 41 pixel touch reach, a 32 pixel body reach, a 2.5 second cooldown, patrol at 35 pixels per second, and the tilapia's bump (160 pixels per second, 0.6 seconds). Meet range 180 pixels; touching it gives a gentle bump. It roots along the bed, and phase 2 gives it a route near the bed, so with its chase it threatens only the low water. For B10, S = 120: Common farther than 180 pixels, Rare more than 120 and at most 180, Epic more than 60 and at most 120, and Legendary at most 60. Its warning cue is a nose-down tip and a small mud puff, with an ink outline in greyscale, shown still and fading under reduced motion; its strike a slow, wide tail sweep along the aim locked at the end of the warning. Its art is friendly, round, and bronze-gold, with two short barbels, a closed mouth, and no teeth, never shown biting or holding the axolotl ([ADR 0007](../adr/0007-kid-safety-and-privacy.md)). Its few frames are baked at boot with the other predators', a residual in [ADR 0021](../adr/0021-danger-pass-chase-rule-and-moving-hazards.md). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 254 | Is the common carp fact card true (C13)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-7): the title "Meet the carp" and the body in C13, shown gently the first time the player meets a carp, with its sources in C13 and source comments in `src/logic/factCards.ts`; no source is invented, and the copy did not change. Owner to verify: the egg claim rests on two secondary sources (National Geographic Kids and The Wildlife Society), and no primary study of carp eating axolotl eggs in Xochimilco was found; "big fish" is not checked against a size source. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 255 | How does the moving hazard type work (N3, A1, A2, A4)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-8; [ADR 0021](../adr/0021-danger-pass-chase-rule-and-moving-hazards.md)): a type holds its id, name, look id, timing (rest, warn, jab, hold, and lift, which fill its period), depth below the surface, shaft half width, touch reach, meet range, and fact card; a spawn holds its id, type, x, offset in seconds, and an optional depth of its own. `movingHazardSpawn()` refuses a warning under the 0.8 second floor, a negative time, a period of 0, or a depth not above 0. A straight shaft comes down from the surface (y 80) at its x; at rest and during the warning it touches nothing. The axolotl's body centre within the touch reach of the shaft, from the surface to the tip, is a touch: one hit by the shared hit rules (A4, A5, A11, A22), from the tip, counted in legs lost (Star 3), never a bump. Each encounter step checks the predators, then still hazards, then moving hazards, then the bump, with at most one hit per step; the touch is checked at the hazard time the step reaches, the same time the view draws. Its clock is the encounter's own hazard time, present only when the area has moving hazards, so every encounter state today stays the same: it starts at 0 each trip and runs only in encounter steps, so it stops with the pause, the banking card, and the carry home. It is not a predator, so it is outside A1's one-at-a-time rule, a residual in ADR 0021; its fixed cycle is always on show, and phase 2 keeps each sweep farther than every predator's lunge range from that predator's loop. It is a B10 threat (its full sweep, S its touch reach), met within its meet range of its sweep outside the safe area, a shape for the gap measure kept out by its touch reach, 80 pixels from every snack start, and a moving threat for the danger bar and a danger part. Looks are data: `MOVING_HAZARD_LOOKS` in `src/art/movingHazardTextures.ts` maps a look id to its painters, the view throws for a look with none baked, and the boot bakes a look only when some area has it. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 256 | What is the punting pole (N3, A2)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-9), in no shipped area yet: `PUNTING_POLES`, a 4 second cycle of rest 1.6 seconds, warn 1.0, jab 0.3, hold 0.5, and lift 0.6; depth 180 pixels, so the tip reaches y 260; half width 5 pixels, a 10 pixel pole; touch reach 18 pixels, the net reach and the body's half height; meet range 180 pixels. So the danger zone is x ± 23 pixels from the surface down to y 278: in an area two screens tall everything below y 278 is safe from it, and in an area one screen tall the 18 pixel band just over the bed passes under it. Its look is a trajinera's bright, painted bow on the surface over the pole, with no people, and the pole. Its warning cue: the boat's shadow darkens on the water under the bow, and the pole's tip dips to the surface with a ripple ring; in greyscale the shadow and the ring have an ink outline, and under reduced motion they show still while the jab still moves. As drawing only, the view shows the resting tip 24 pixels above the surface and dips it in the warning. It is never shown hitting or pinning the axolotl. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 257 | Is the punting pole fact card true (C13)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-10): id `punting-pole`, the title "Trajineras", and the body in C13, with its sources in C13 and source comments in `src/logic/factCards.ts`; no source is invented, and the copy did not change. Owner to verify: "flat-bottomed" rests on Wikipedia (its Fodor's source returned 403); the same Wikipedia page says a rower uses an oar, while the other sources describe poles; and "the mud" is the sources' canal floor, sediment, and marshy bottom. The UNESCO World Heritage listing returned 403 and was not read. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 258 | How do drifting ghost nets work (N3, A9)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-11), in no shipped area yet: a ghost net drifts when its data has a drift (dx, dy, period, offset), back and forth from its place to its place plus (dx, dy), its share of the path (1 − cos(2π (t + offset) / period)) / 2, where t is the trip's element time; a net with no drift never moves. `DRIFTING_NETS`: a 16 second cycle and a path of at most 200 pixels; `netDrift()` refuses a longer path or a period of 0. A 160 pixel path averages 20 pixels per second and peaks at about 31. It snags like a still net, measured where it is now, never costs a leg, and is not a B10 threat; it is met within 100 pixels of where it is now. The snag and the meeting use the element time the step reaches. Every placement rule uses its whole drift path (its shape's bounding rect over the path): snack starts, mobile snack lanes and spots, the gap measure, and phase 2's net rules (190.5 pixels from every current, the predator clearances, the spots, the stranding places, and the exit). The mats' push-out checks each net where it is now. Phase 2 lays each drift path along the flow of the current nearby. Under reduced motion it still drifts, with no extra sway; in greyscale it looks the same as a still net. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 259 | What are the danger bar and the ramp bar, and how are they measured (N2)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-12), a key default for the owner to decide, with one part open. The full bar, from Area 4 on: at least ⌈0.75 x screens⌉ moving threats, where each tilapia route, other predator route, and moving hazard is one, and the screens are the area's width over 480 times its height over 360; at least 2 predator species and 1 moving hazard type; every predator chasing; and the longest quiet stretch of the main path at most 480 pixels. The ramp bar, Areas 2 and 3: the tilapia chase, the longest quiet stretch at most 480 pixels, and no moving hazard type in Area 2 and at most one in Area 3. Area 1 stays gentle. In reach means within lunge range plus leash of a predator's loop (no leash without a chase or with warn on route), or within touch reach of a moving hazard's sweep, the main path sampled every 1 pixel. `src/logic/dangerBar.ts` has the counts, the quiet stretch, and both bar checks; its tests use made-up layouts and the dev area, and assert nothing about Areas 1 to 5. The dev area does not meet the full bar (6 moving threats of the 9 its 12 screens need, 3 predator species, 1 moving hazard type, and a longest quiet stretch of 899.16 pixels): it is a showcase, not a bar proof. Open: the issue asks for a "short leash" in the ramp bar but gives no number, so the ramp bar checks that the tilapia chase but not a leash length; the other option is a number, for example at most `TILAPIA_RAMP_CHASE`'s 80 pixels. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 260 | How does the one-new-thing rule treat the danger parts (N2)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-13), a key default for the owner to decide: the danger parts are outside the rule. Counting new things per area leaves out a species marked as a danger part (the carp) and every moving hazard type, so each area's one-new-thing pins and its limit stay as they are when phase 2 adds danger parts. The mark is on the species, or comes with the type, never a shared list of ids. The danger parts still count as threats, have fact cards, and are met. Mobile snack types are not counted either (open question 274). The other option: count them, so an area adding the carp or a moving hazard type would use its one new hazard or predator. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 261 | How does phase 2 add the danger parts to an area (N2)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-14): each area's own files only. A tilapia chase goes in the area's own tilapia tuning in its layout file, `{ ...<its tuning or TILAPIA>, chase }`; Areas 2 and 3 get a `tilapia` field for the first time, and with only `chase` added their risk bands do not change. Any other species chases, or takes per-area tuning, through `tunedSpecies(SPECIES, { ...TUNING, chase })` in the area's layout file, keeping its id. Moving hazards: the area's own `movingHazards` list, built with `movingHazardSpawn()`. Drifting nets: `drift: netDrift(...)` on the area's own ghost net data. Mobile snacks: `mobileSnacks: mobileSnackMix({...})` in the area's own layout file, with its worms and bugs lowered by the mobile count; its area entry in `src/logic/areas.ts` stays as it is. Proofs live in the area's own layout test (the bars, the gaps, its snack counts, its snack ramp row, the clear check on every lane and spot, its magnet pull, and no one-time darters with shrimp), its own fairness file, and its own golden, re-recorded on purpose. Each phase 2 issue edits only its own N2 paragraph, its rows, the snack values it built, and new open questions; never the H4 snack ramp table. The only shared code edit in phase 2 is a pond look in Area 3 (issue #120): one painter, one look entry, one moving hazard type, and its card. The Collection Book pins move with whichever issue first adds the carp, the pole, the tadpole, or the shrimp to an area, and the issue that merges second rebases and fixes them. Needs owner confirmation. |

The Home Spot at (120, 200), radius 72; the safe area x 40 to 300 from the surface to 678; the exit at (2780, 625), radius 40. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 263 | How are Areas 1 to 5 kept unchanged by the danger pass (Testing Decisions)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-16): a golden test for Area 5, `src/logic/chinampa2Golden.test.ts`, modelled on the Area 4 golden, recorded from main in the first commit, before any shared change. | 264 | How does the fairness check handle the chase and moving hazards (B10)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-17): the shared helpers move into a pure module, `src/logic/riskFairness.ts`, and the per-area blocks into one file per area (`canalFairness`, `pondFairness`, `pond2Fairness`, `chinampaFairness`, and `chinampa2Fairness`), with every test name and assertion kept, so the test count does not change; `src/logic/riskFairness.test.ts` keeps only tests of the harness. A safe start is farther than lunge range from the loop of every predator that does not chase, farther than leash plus the larger of sight and lunge range from the loop of every predator that chases (the leash as written, also for a warn on route species), and outside every moving hazard's sweep grown by its touch reach. The start times span each moving hazard's cycle too. Visits step the real encounter, so chases and poles play. With no chase and no moving hazard, every result is the same as before. | 265 | How do the scenes draw the danger parts and mobile snacks (N3, H4)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-18): the tilapia view draws the tilapia only, so a new species never gets a tilapia sprite, and its poof and halo handle a hit from a moving hazard. A carp view, like the garter snake's: swim frames while the carp moves, the warning pose with the mud puff, the sweep pose, turning away in the cooldown, and one pose under reduced motion. A moving hazard view reuses its bow, shadow, shaft, and ripple ring, placed each frame from the hazard's phase and tip depth. A mobile snack view sets each tadpole's and shrimp's frame and facing each frame, with every snack sprite kept in one map, so the pop and the last-3 stars work unchanged. The ghost net view moves drifting nets each frame by the element time. The swim scene builds each new view only when its area has that part, and Areas 1 to 5 build and call nothing new. The boot bakes the carp frames with the other predators', and the moving hazard looks and mobile snack textures only when some area, or the loaded dev area, needs them. The moving hazard and ghost net views allocate one small object per moving or drifting spawn per frame through the logic helpers; only areas with those parts pay it, and no shipped area has them (a residual in [ADR 0021](../adr/0021-danger-pass-chase-rule-and-moving-hazards.md)). Needs owner confirmation. |

The copy fit test checks the four cards directly, so phase 2 never edits it for copy fit. Needs owner confirmation. |

| 269 | What are the mobile snacks' ids, names, and modules (H4)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-22; [ADR 0021](../adr/0021-danger-pass-chase-rule-and-moving-hazards.md)): the snack kinds gain `tadpole` and `shrimp`, with spawn ids `snack-tadpole-<n>` and `snack-shrimp-<n>`, n from 1 in data order. `src/logic/mobileSnacks.ts` holds the rules and their constructors (`tadpoleRule()`, `tadpoleLane()`, `shrimpRule()`, and `mobileSnackMix()`), the lane a tadpole carries, the clear check, the top speed and magnet pull, the one-time darter choice, meeting, the drawn pose, and the snack ramp (`mobileSnackShare()`, `SNACK_RAMP`, and `meetsSnackRamp()`). `src/logic/shrimpFlick.ts` holds the repeat flick. The limits are `TADPOLES` and `SHRIMP` in `src/logic/tuning.ts`, with no per-area values. The fact cards are `TADPOLE_FACT` (id `tadpole`, "Tadpole") and `SHRIMP_FACT` (id `shrimp`, "Freshwater shrimp"), with `TADPOLE_MEET_RANGE` and `SHRIMP_MEET_RANGE`. The area data field is `mobileSnacks`, copied onto the built layout only when set. Also: `meetSnacks` in `src/logic/trip.ts`, an optional `keepClear` on the spit rule in `src/logic/plasticWorms.ts`, and an optional `lane` on a snack. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 270 | How are mobile snacks set and counted per area (H4)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-23): fixed area data written out as numbers in the area's layout file, tadpole lanes and shrimp spots, never seeded, so building a zone makes no new random draw. They come after the seeded worms and bugs, tadpoles first, then shrimp, in data order, and the seeded snacks keep their ids, indexes, and places, so their drift and bob do not change. An area without mobile snacks builds exactly as before. A mix with lanes and no tadpole rule, spots and no shrimp rule, or any value outside its limits is refused. Each area keeps its total snack count, and phase 2 lowers its worms and bugs by its mobile count; the 4 snacks in the current are always worms or bugs. The phase 2 counts are in the H4 snack ramp table. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 271 | How does the tadpole swim (H4)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-24): a straight lane from (x, y) to (x + dx, y + dy), 80 to 160 pixels long (120 in Areas 2 to 5), swum there and back at a steady speed, turning at once at each end. At time t on the snack drift clock, which runs only on swimming steps and starts at 0 each trip, its distance along a lane of length L is min(s, 2L − s), where s = ((t + offset) x speed) mod 2L, clamped to the swim bounds. The stored place is the lane start, so the Snack magnet moves the lane with it. No drift and no bob, the same with and without reduced motion. Lane n of N gets offset (n − 1) x (2L / speed) / N. It faces the sign of its x velocity, and right on a lane with dx = 0. The wiggle is drawing only: 4 tail frames at 8 frames a second from the scene clock and a ±8 degree sway; under reduced motion, frame 0 with no sway. Its speed is above 0 and at most 72 pixels per second, and an offset outside [0, 2L / speed) is refused. It comes back to each lane end every 2L / speed seconds. Speeds: Area 2, 25 pixels per second; Area 3, 35; Area 4, 48; Area 5, 60; Area 6 on, 60 to 72, never slower than the area before. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 272 | How does the freshwater shrimp flick (H4)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-25): at rest it drifts and bobs like a worm or a bug, faces the head, and is ready at the trip start. A ready, uncollected shrimp drawn within its trigger radius of the head reach centre (inclusive, but not on it) flicks straight away from the head by its flick distance, only when its drift and bob box at the landing passes the clear check (open question 273); else it stays ready. The flick moves its stored place, gliding at its flick speed, or landing at once under reduced motion. It then rests, a ready-at time on the drift clock, and is ready again, with no limit. A collected shrimp never moves, and a flick whose shrimp is eaten stops; a magnet pull adds to a glide. Limits (`SHRIMP`): a trigger radius above 36 pixels, a rest of at least 1.0 second, a flick of 24 to 72 pixels, a flick speed above 0 and at most 150 pixels per second, and the catch limit rest x 70 ≥ trigger radius + flick distance − 36, where 70 is a one-leg axolotl's 90 less 20 for drift (every area's drift is under 16). Values, with a 60 pixel trigger in every area: Area 2, 2.0 seconds, 36 pixels, 100 pixels per second; Area 3, 1.7, 44, 115; Area 4, 1.4, 52, 130; Area 5, 1.2, 60, 145; Area 6 on, Area 5's or harder, inside the catch limit, 145 to 150 pixels per second. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 273 | Where may a mobile snack go (H4)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-26), a key default for the owner to decide: one shared check. A tadpole's whole lane, and a shrimp's whole drift and bob box at its spot or at a flick landing, must be inside the swim bounds and below the highest wave; outside the Home Spot ring, the safe area, every current, and every mat pair's whole sweep, edges counting as inside; at least 80 pixels from every plastic worm where it is now, every hazard's shape (a drifting net by its whole drift path), every element sweep, and every moving hazard's sweep; and farther than S from every B10 threat: each predator route by its lunge range from its loop (a tilapia by the area's own tuning), with no leash added, and each hazard that costs a leg by its reach. Phase 2 proves every lane and spot with it, and each flick landing is checked at run time with the plastic worms where they are now. A spat plastic worm never lands within 80 pixels of a tadpole lane, and nothing changes without lanes. A magnet-pulled tadpole's lane moves with it and can briefly cross a keep-out, a residual in [ADR 0021](../adr/0021-danger-pass-chase-rule-and-moving-hazards.md); the pull is faster than every snack, so it is eaten soon. The other option: S + leash for a chasing predator, which leaves much less room in full-bar areas. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 274 | How do mobile snacks count, and what happens to the one-time darters (H4, H2, H3)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-27), a key default for the owner to decide: a tadpole and a shrimp each count as one snack for the counter, the last-3 sparkle, the exit, the snack speed-up, and the reset when carried home. The shrimp replaces the one-time darter: an area whose layout has shrimp gets no one-time darters, and otherwise the darters are picked from the worms and bugs only, so a tadpole is never one, and Areas 1 to 5 get exactly today's darter ids. The one-time darter rule, its data, and its tests do not change; phase 2 leaves the area's one-time darter data in its area entry, and the shrimp turns it off. Mobile snack types are snacks, not threats or elements, so the one-new-thing rule and the danger bar never count them. The other option: each phase 2 issue deletes the one-time darter line from its area entry, which is a shared-file edit. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 275 | How fast does the Snack magnet pull in an area with mobile snacks (A19)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-28), a key default for the owner to decide: each trip's pull is max(base, 1.2 x the fastest top speed of the area's mobile snacks), with base the perk's 40 pixels per second. A tadpole's top speed is its speed, and a shrimp's flick distance / (rest + flick distance / flick speed), the farthest it gets per second over one rest and one flick; a flick on its own is a short hop like a darter's. A pulled snack always closes on a still head. The swim scene works it out once per trip from the zone. The perk's constant and its tests do not change, and an area with no mobile snacks gets exactly 40, so Areas 1 to 5 and their goldens are unchanged. Values: Area 2, 40 (1.2 x 25 = 30 is lower); Area 3, 42; Area 4, 57.6; Area 5, 72; the dev area, 72. The 72 pixel per second tadpole limit keeps every pull at or under 86.4, below a one-leg axolotl's 90. The other option: one bigger pull for every area, which would change the perk constant and its tests. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 276 | What are the mobile snack fact cards, and when are they met (C13, C14, A21)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-29): "Meet the tadpole" (id `tadpole`, "Tadpole") and "Freshwater shrimp" (id `shrimp`, "Freshwater shrimp"), with the bodies and sources in C13 and source comments in `src/logic/factCards.ts`; no source is invented, and the copy did not change. The axolotl meets a type the first time its centre is within 120 pixels of an uncollected snack of that type, where it is drawn, outside the safe area; the id joins the trip's met list, so the card comes due and shows with play paused, as in A21. An area's snack type cards come after its element cards, tadpole first. Owner to verify, on the shrimp card: "tiny" (the source lists a size of 7 cm), and the sources are about Australian species, not species of Xochimilco. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 277 | How do the tadpole and the shrimp look (H4)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-30; [art style guide](../art-style-guide.md)): the tadpole, a round, dark olive-brown body with a lighter belly, one big friendly eye with a highlight, and a long thin tail fin, about as big as a worm snack, with 4 tail frames; the shrimp, a small, curved, pale blue-grey see-through body with soft bands, two long antennae, a dot eye, and a fan tail, with a rest frame and a flick frame with the tail tucked under. Both have the usual ink outline, and in greyscale they read apart from the worm, the bug, and the plastic worm by shape alone: the tadpole by its round head and long tail, the shrimp by its curve, antennae, and fan tail. Neither is ever shown hurt; eating one shows the usual snack pop. Under reduced motion the tadpole shows frame 0 with no sway and slides along its lane, and the shrimp shows its rest frame and its flick lands at once. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 278 | What is the snack ramp, and how is it proved (H4, N2)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-31; owner decision on issue #117, 2026-10-23, for the ramp itself): H4's snack ramp table holds the starting values, the counts, tadpole speeds, shrimp values, and magnet pulls of open questions 270, 271, 272, and 275, and N2's danger pass records it beside the danger bar, as a rule every area issue from Area 2 on must meet. `SNACK_RAMP` has rows for Areas 1 to 5 and one rule for Area 6 on, and `meetsSnackRamp(number, layout)` checks: Area 1, no mobile snacks; the mobile share, Area 2 15 to 25%, Area 3 30 to 40%, Area 4 45 to 55%, Area 5 55 to 65%, and Area 6 on 55 to 70%; the tadpole speed, Area 2 20 to 30 pixels per second, Area 3 30 to 40, Area 4 40 to 55, Area 5 55 to 65, and Area 6 on 60 to 72; and, from Area 2 on, both types. A shared test over the area list checks that Area 1 has none and that, by area number among areas that have them, the share, tadpole speed, flick distance, and flick speed never go down and the rest never goes up; it holds today, with no area having any, and checks each phase 2 issue as it merges. Phase 2 proves its own row in its own layout test, and issue #117 asserts nothing about a shipped area's row. Needs owner confirmation. |
