### Tuning values (starting points)

| Value | Starting point |
|---|---|
| Regrowth time | About 20 seconds per leg |
| Protected time | About 2 seconds |
| Telegraph wind-up | At least 0.8 seconds |
| Lunge cooldown | About 2.5 seconds (2.2 for Area 4's tilapia, and 2 for Area 5's tilapia and the garter snake) |
| Top speed by legs (4 / 3 / 2 / 1) | 100% / 90% / 75% / 60%. One leg must still beat the strongest current. |
| Tilapia in the canal | At least 3 |
| Tilapia in the pond | 4, with the closest routes 290 pixels apart |
| Area 3 size | 6 x 1 screens, 2880 x 360 world pixels |
| Tilapia in Area 3 | 4, with the closest routes 290 pixels apart, all before the herons' stretches |
| Area 4 size | 6 x 2 screens, 2880 x 720 world pixels, bed top at y 678. The Home Spot at (120, 200), its safe area x 40 to 300 from the surface to the bed; the exit at (2780, 625), radius 40 (open question 191). |
| Tilapia in Area 4 | 2, more than twice Area 4's lunge range apart: one loop over the root tangle, x 420 to 580 and y 460 to 500, and one tall loop from the top row into the bottom row, x 2500 to 2660 and y 300 to 470 |
| Tilapia tuning in Area 4 (the predator ladder) | Warning (wind-up) 0.85 seconds, with the 0.8 second floor. Strike speed 400 pixels per second (380 in Areas 1 to 3), lasting 0.4 seconds. Lunge range 145 pixels (140). Cooldown 2.2 seconds (2.5). Patrol speed 50 pixels per second (45). Every other value as Areas 1 to 3. Provisional (N2, N3; open question 192). |
| Area 5 size | 7 x 2 screens, 3360 x 720 world pixels, bed top at y 678. The Home Spot at (120, 200), its safe area x 40 to 300 from the surface down to y 360 (the top row only); the exit at (120, 625), radius 40, straight under the Home Spot. 10 treasure slots; the special treasure the Piñata (open questions 222 and 238). |
| Tilapia in Area 5 | 2, more than twice Area 5's lunge range apart: one loop low in the bottom row, x 2160 to 2260 and y 560 to 620, and one in the top right, x 3140 to 3250 and y 280 to 320 |
| Tilapia tuning in Area 5 (the predator ladder) | Warning (wind-up) 0.85 seconds, Area 4's, with the 0.8 second floor. Strike speed 420 pixels per second, lasting 0.4 seconds. Lunge range 150 pixels. Cooldown 2 seconds. Patrol speed 55 pixels per second. Every other value as Areas 1 to 3. Provisional (N2, N3; open question 232). |
| Garter snake stretches | Two straight stretches at y 100, the snake's head line, each 200 pixels long: `garter-snake-0` from x 1040 to 1240 and `garter-snake-1` from x 2600 to 2800, in open top-row water (open question 230). |
| Garter snake tuning | Warning (wind-up) 0.8 seconds, the floor. Dart (strike) 360 pixels per second for 0.35 seconds, 126 pixels, along the aim locked at the end of the warning. Reach (lunge range) 110 pixels from its head, so S = 110 for the risk ladder (B10). Cooldown 2 seconds, swimming its route. Patrol speed 55 pixels per second. Hit reach 26, touch reach 29, body reach, bump speed, and bump cooldown: the tilapia's. Warn on route tolerance: 2 pixels from its stretch, inclusive (open question 247). Depth: warnings at y 212 or less, hits at y 254 or less, the head at y 228 or less, and y 260 and below safe. Meet range (fact card): 180 pixels from its head. Provisional (N3; open questions 230 and 231). |
| Tilapia lunge hit reach | 26 pixels from centre to centre (was 30), the same at every size (F4) |
| Tilapia touch reach (bump, no leg lost) | 29 pixels from centre to centre (was 34), the same at every size (F4) |
| Axolotl size scale (Small / Medium / Large) | 0.72 / 0.85 / 1.00. Default: Medium. Looks only (F4). |
| Treasures | Canal: 12 or more Decorations and Wearables (at least 4 Decorations and 8 Wearables), plus 12 Paint shells, 24 in all. Pond: 12 more (4 Decorations, 7 Wearables, and 1 Paint shell). Area 3: 12 more (6 Decorations, 5 Wearables, and 1 Paint shell). Area 4: 12 more (6 Decorations, 5 Wearables, and 1 Paint shell). Area 5: 12 more (5 Decorations, 6 Wearables, and 1 Paint shell). Whole catalogue: 72, which are 25 Decorations, 31 Wearables, and 16 Paint shells (B3). |
| Treasure slots per trip | 6 in the canal, 7 in the pond, 8 in Area 3, 9 in Area 4 (open question 220 asks whether Area 4's often stay empty), and 10 in Area 5 (open question 238 asks the same), plus any lost treasures and the special treasure while it is unbanked, as extras on top of the slots (B4) |
| Rarity chance per slot (Common / Rare / Epic / Legendary) | 65% / 27% / 6% / 2%. A slot whose rarity has none left takes a more common rarity with one left, nearest first, then only the next rarer one, else it stays empty (B4, issue #111). |
| Hiding spot weighting | 1 for the spot nearest the main swim path to 3 for the farthest, linear within each treasure's pool |
| Risk bands: route distance (Common / Rare / Epic / Legendary) | More than 200 / more than 140 and at most 200 / more than 70 and at most 140 / at most 70 pixels from the nearest tilapia route (B10). Lunge range: 140 pixels. Other threats use the generic rule in B10 (bands relative to each threat's strike range); the tilapia's values are unchanged. In Area 4, whose tilapia have their own lunge range of 145 pixels: more than 205 / more than 145 and at most 205 / more than 72.5 and at most 145 / at most 72.5 pixels. In Area 5, whose tilapia have their own lunge range of 150 pixels: more than 210 / more than 150 and at most 210 / more than 75 and at most 150 / at most 75 pixels; from a garter snake's stretch, with S = 110: more than 170 / more than 110 and at most 170 / more than 55 and at most 110 / at most 55 pixels. |
| Turn clearance | Every hiding spot at least 60 pixels from every tilapia route waypoint (B10). Any predator turn uses the same 60 pixels under the generic rule in B10, such as both ends of a heron's or a garter snake's stretch; the tilapia's value is unchanged. |
| Risk weight | 1 to 2, linear in route distance within each treasure's pool, 2 at the rarity's risky side (nearer for Rare, Epic, and Legendary; farther for Common). Multiplied by the hiding spot weighting (B10). |
| Per-area tuning | Every area uses these treasure slot counts and chances unless it sets its own, keyed by area id |
| Stranded axolotl chance | 40% of trips, about 4 in 10, the default for every area that sets no chance of its own, unless one is already waiting (C2, C5; issue #111) |
| Snack regrowth speed-up | Each snack adds 20% of one leg's regrowth to the leg that is regrowing (about 4 seconds at the base time). Never past a full leg. |
| Snack eat reach | A circle of 36 pixels radius, centred 25 pixels ahead of the axolotl's middle along its body, turning with its tilt. While the body squashes in a turn, the centre moves in with it. The same at every size. It covers the whole head of the largest size; smaller sizes reach a little in front of the snout. |
| Treasure hint range (Common / Rare / Epic / Legendary) | 150 / 115 / 85 / 60 pixels. Rarer is shorter. |
| Dash pot touch distance | 30 pixels |
| Dream colours by rarity | Common: Mint, Sky blue, Lavender (solid). Rare: Sunset orange, Bubblegum, Midnight blue, and, in the pond, Lily green, in Area 3, Firefly gold, in Area 4, Poppy red, and, in Area 5, Plum (solid). Epic: Hearts, Tie-dye, Iridescent (patterned or sheen). Legendary: Rainbow, Galaxy, Tie-dye galaxy (patterned). |
| Speed perk (Common / Rare / Epic / Legendary) | +4% / +6% / +8% / +10% top speed. Cap: +15% in total. |
| Regrowth perk (Common / Rare / Epic / Legendary) | 10% / 15% / 20% / 25% less regrowth time. Cap: 35% less in total. |
| Dash perk (Common / Rare / Epic / Legendary) | +10% / +15% / +20% / +25% Tail Dash length: each dash lasts longer. The recharge stays the same. Cap: +35% in total. |
| Treasure sense perk (Common / Rare / Epic / Legendary) | +15% / +25% / +35% / +50% hint range. Cap: +60% in total. |
| Snack magnet perk (Common / Rare / Epic / Legendary) | 30 / 40 / 50 / 60 pixels pull radius. Cap: 70 pixels in total. |
| Snack magnet pull speed | 40 pixels per second toward the explorer's head, the same for every radius, in an area with no mobile snacks. About 3 times the fastest Area 1 snack drift, at least twice the Area 2, Area 3, Area 4, and Area 5 drift, and well under the slowest swim (90 pixels per second with one leg). In an area with mobile snacks (H4), max(40, 1.2 x the top speed of its fastest mobile snack), worked out once per trip: a tadpole's top speed is its speed, and a shrimp's is flick distance / (rest + flick distance / flick speed). The perk's 40 does not change. With the 72 pixel per second tadpole limit, every pull is at most 86.4 pixels per second, under one leg's 90. Phase 2 values in the H4 snack ramp table; the dev area's is 72 (A19; open question 275). |
| Snack drift (canal / pond / Area 3 / Area 4 / Area 5) | The same amplitudes, 16 pixels across and 8 up and down, with periods of 10 and 8 seconds in the canal, 8 and 6.4 seconds in the pond, 7.8 and 6.24 seconds in Area 3, 7.6 and 6.08 seconds in Area 4, and 7.5 and 6.0 seconds in Area 5. Peak speed about 11.9 pixels per second in the canal, about 14.8 in the pond, about 15.2 in Area 3, about 15.6 in Area 4, and about 15.8 in Area 5. Kept at most 20 pixels per second, half the Snack magnet pull, and below the headway a one-leg axolotl makes against the area's strongest current: 20 pixels per second in the pond, 16 in Area 3, 18 in Area 4, and 17 in Area 5 (H4). |
| Snacks in the pond | 20: 12 worms and 8 bugs, 4 of them in the stream. Every snack starts at least 80 pixels from every hazard's shape (N3). |
| Pond current | The stream at the right end flows left at 70 pixels per second. |
| Plastic worms in the pond | 6. Each starts outside the safe area, never under a lily pad, more than 140 pixels from every tilapia route, and at least 371.5 pixels from every current (N3). |
| Plastic worm tuning | Queasy top speed: 0.6 of the top speed (54 pixels per second with one leg). Queasy time: 3 seconds, no stacking. Spit distance: 48 pixels ahead of the head reach centre. Meet range (fact card): 100 pixels. Wobble: 0.08 radians, one cycle every 0.6 seconds, none under reduced motion. Touch reach: the snack eat reach. Provisional (N3). |
| Snacks in Area 3 | 24: 14 worms and 10 bugs, 4 of them in the stream. Every snack starts at least 80 pixels from every plastic worm (N3). |
| Area 3 current | The stream flows left at 74 pixels per second, from x 1880 to 2240 and y 120 to 280. A one-leg axolotl makes 16 pixels per second of headway against it (A9). |
| Stream mouth (art) | At the right edge of the screen column that holds the current's right end (x 2400 in Area 3). A mouth in open water, not at the area's right edge, fades the stream band and its flow marks out over the last 40 pixels before it, and the next screen draws the mouth stones that spill past the edge (open question 172). |
| Plastic worms in Area 3 | 5. Each starts outside the safe area, never under a lily pad, more than 140 pixels from every tilapia route, out of every heron's reach, and at least 371.5 pixels from every current (N3). |
| Stranding places in Area 3 | 6, each at least 80 pixels from every tilapia route and from every heron's stretch, and out of every heron's reach. |
| Heron stretches | Two straight stretches at y 120, the line where each heron's beak rests, each 180 pixels long: `heron-0` from x 2190 to 2370, over the spots past the stream, and `heron-1` from x 2440 to 2620, over the root arch (open question 214). A heron warns at no axolotl deeper than y 230, and its stab hits no axolotl centred below about y 256. The bottom band, from y 230 down to the sea bed top at y 318, gets no warning (open question 208). |
| Heron tuning | Warning (wind-up) 1.2 seconds, with a 0.8 second floor. Stab (strike) 0.3 seconds, from where it stands to the spot fixed at the warning start; strike speed 110 / 0.3, about 367 pixels per second, the stab's top speed, read by no heron rule. Reach (strike range) 110 pixels from where it stands, so S = 110 for the risk ladder (B10). Hit reach 26 pixels, the tilapia's Medium reach (F4). Cooldown 3 seconds, standing still. Wading speed 20 pixels per second, so a wade there and back along a stretch takes 18 seconds. Touch reach, body reach, and bump speed 0: it never touches or bumps. The heron's step on the epic #32 predator ladder is 1.2 seconds, 110 pixels, and 3 seconds (N3). Provisional (N3, issue #111; open questions 165 and 213). |
| Heron meet range (fact card) | 180 pixels from where the heron stands, at any time, the same as the tilapia's; larger than its 110 pixel reach, so the card can show before the first stab. Cover does not stop a meeting (open question 216). |
| Snack darting (Areas 3, 4, and 5) | 3 darters in each, picked by the area data with no random draw. Trigger: within 60 pixels of the head reach centre, outside the 36 pixel eat reach. Hop: 40 pixels straight away from the head, at 120 pixels per second (a third of a second), landing at once under reduced motion. Once per trip each. Provisional (H4; open questions 165 and 191). |
| Snacks in Area 4 | 28: 16 worms and 12 bugs, 4 of them in the current. Every snack starts at least 80 pixels from every ghost net (N3). |
| Area 4 current | Water flows left at 72 pixels per second through the gap between two chinampas in the bottom row, from x 2140 to 2340 and y 380 to 600. A one-leg axolotl makes 18 pixels per second of headway against it (A9). |
| Ghost nets in Area 4 | 5 rectangles: two in the bottom row, with the bottom-row way between them (x 1000 to 1060, at y 400 to 470 and 570 to 640); two in the top row, with the main path's dive between them (x 1460 to 1510 and 1610 to 1660, y 260 to 360); and one near the surface on the way into the top-right bank (x 2200 to 2260, y 94 to 170). Each sits outside the safe area, clear of the ring and the exit, farther than a lunge from every tilapia route, and at least 190.5 pixels from every current (N3; open question 195). |
| Ghost net tuning | Snagged top speed: 0.5 of the top speed (45 pixels per second with one leg). Snag time: 2 seconds, on entry only, no stacking. Reach: 18 pixels from the net's edge to the body centre (open question 194). Meet range (fact card): 100 pixels from the net's edge. Wriggle: 0.1 radians, one cycle every 0.4 seconds, none under reduced motion. Net to current clearance: at least 190.5 pixels (2 seconds x 150 x 1.15 x 0.5 = 172.5, plus the 18 pixel reach). Provisional (N3; open question 193). |
| Stranding places in Area 4 | 6, each at least 80 pixels from every tilapia route, clear of every ghost net by the net reach plus the rescue reach, and clear of the spots, the current, and the cave. |
| Hiding spots in Area 4 | 6 Common and 6 Rare under the banks (B5), the Commons near the main path and the Rares in the top-right dead end; 3 Epic and 3 Legendary (in Dash pots) in the root tangle; and 2 Epic and 2 Legendary (in Dash pots) just past the current. Each pool is larger than its Area 4 treasures (4 Common, 4 Rare, 2 Epic, and 2 Legendary). |
| Snacks in Area 5 | 32: 18 worms and 14 bugs, 4 of them in the current. Every snack starts at least 80 pixels from every ghost net and every mat pair's sweep (N3; open question 236). |
| Area 5 current | Water flows right at 73 pixels per second through the gap between two chinampas in the bottom row, from x 2460 to 2660 and y 380 to 640, against the main path. A one-leg axolotl makes 17 pixels per second of headway against it (A9; open question 236). |
| Ghost nets in Area 5 | 5 rectangles: one in the top row over the second mat pair (x 600 to 700, y 240 to 280); two in the bottom row with the main path between them (x 1080 to 1140, at y 372 to 420 and 504 to 570); one in the bottom row under the first mat pair (x 1540 to 1640, y 446 to 516); and one in the bottom right by the turn (x 2880 to 2940, y 480 to 560). Each sits outside the safe area, clear of the ring and the exit, farther from each predator's route than its strike reach plus the net reach and the safe passing width, at least 190.5 pixels from every current, and clear of every mat sweep by the net reach plus one body (N3; open question 233). |
| Narrowest main-path gap | Area 3: about 136.09 pixels. Area 4: 64 pixels, between two nets. Area 5: 48 pixels, between `chinampa-2/ghost-net-2` and `chinampa-2/ghost-net-3` at x 1110. Never under the 36 pixel safe passing width (N2; open question 234). |
| One-leg time to the exit in Area 5 | 76.3 seconds along the main path from the mats' time 0, waiting at each closed channel; 75.3 to 81.9 seconds over 16 start times, every 0.5 seconds through the 8 second mat cycle. Under the 180 second limit (H1; open question 234). |
| Water hyacinth mat tuning | Cycle 8 seconds: open 3 (the first 1 drifting apart), closing 1 (the warning), closed 4. Open once the clear height is at least 36 pixels, the safe passing width; fully open at least 72 pixels. Body grow: 18 pixels, the net reach. Meet range (fact card): 180 pixels from a pair's sweep. Longest channel: 189 pixels (150 x 0.6 x (3 − 0.5) − 2 x 18). Provisional (N3; open questions 227 and 228). |
| Water hyacinth mats in Area 5 | 2 pairs, each channel 150 pixels long and 72 pixels tall when open. Pair 1 (`chinampa-2/hyacinth-mats-1`) gates the top row: x 1500 to 1650, from the surface to y 360, its channel at y 220, its cycle starting at 0 seconds. Pair 2 (`chinampa-2/hyacinth-mats-2`) gates the bottom row: x 580 to 730, from y 360 to the bed top at 678, its channel at y 500, its cycle starting at 4 seconds (open questions 226 and 227). |
| Stranding places in Area 5 | 6, at (360, 300), (900, 600), (1820, 310), (2200, 280), (2800, 520), and (3200, 560), each at least 80 pixels from every tilapia route and snake stretch, out of every snake's reach, clear of every ghost net by the net reach plus the rescue reach, and clear of the spots, the current, the cave, and every mat sweep (open question 237). |
| Hiding spots in Area 5 | 6 Common and 8 Rare under the banks (B5), at y 110: the Commons near the main path under the first two banks, and the Rares off it, 2 at the right end of the first bank and 6 in the top-right dead end; 2 Epic and 2 Legendary (in Dash pots) in the root tangle; and 2 Epic and 2 Legendary (in Dash pots) just past the current. Each pool is larger than its Area 5 treasures (4 Common, 4 Rare, 2 Epic, and 2 Legendary) (open question 235). |
| Chase limits | Chase speed strictly between a 2-leg axolotl's top speed, 112.5 pixels per second, and a 4-leg axolotl's, 150. Sight greater than the lunge range of the tuning that carries the chase. Leash 0 or more; a warn on route species' leash counts as 0. No chase on a species with a fixed strike spot or a still cooldown (N3; open question 250). |
| Chase defaults (issue #117; none in a shipped area yet) | Tilapia, full bar (Area 4 on): sight 240 pixels, leash 160 pixels, speed 125 pixels per second. Tilapia, ramp bar (Areas 2 and 3): sight 200, leash 80, speed 115; Area 2's leash shorter than Area 3's, for example 60. Garter snake: sight 200, leash 0 (along its stretch), speed 120. Common carp: sight 220, leash 120, speed 115. Heron: none. Provisional (N3; open question 251). |
| Common carp tuning | Warning (wind-up) 1.0 second, with the 0.8 second floor. Sweep (strike) 200 pixels per second for 0.6 seconds, 120 pixels. Reach (lunge range) 120 pixels, so S = 120 for the risk ladder (B10): Common farther than 180 pixels, Rare more than 120 and at most 180, Epic more than 60 and at most 120, and Legendary at most 60. Hit reach 38 pixels, touch reach 41, body reach 32. Cooldown 2.5 seconds. Patrol speed 35 pixels per second. Bump speed 160 and bump cooldown 0.6 seconds, the tilapia's. Meet range (fact card): 180 pixels. Provisional (N3; open question 253). |
| Punting pole tuning | A 4 second cycle: rest 1.6 seconds, warn 1.0 (at least the 0.8 second floor), jab 0.3, hold 0.5, and lift 0.6. Depth 180 pixels below the surface (y 80), so the tip reaches y 260. Half width 5 pixels, a 10 pixel pole. Touch reach 18 pixels, the net reach and the body's half height. So the danger zone is x ± 23 pixels, from the surface down to y 278. Meet range (fact card): 180 pixels from its sweep. Provisional (N3; open question 256). |
| Drifting ghost nets | A 16 second cycle and a path of at most 200 pixels; a path longer than that, or a cycle of 0, is refused. The net's share of its path is (1 − cos(2π (t + offset) / period)) / 2, with t the trip's element time. A 160 pixel path averages 20 pixels per second and peaks at about 31. Reach, snag, and meet range as a still ghost net's, measured where the net is now. Provisional (N3; open question 258). |
| Danger bar (full bar, from Area 4 on) | At least ⌈0.75 x screens⌉ moving threats (screens = width / 480 x height / 360; 9 for a 6 x 2 area), at least 2 predator species, at least 1 moving hazard type, every predator chasing, and a longest quiet stretch of the main path of at most 480 pixels, sampled every pixel. In reach: within lunge range plus leash of a predator's loop, or within touch reach of a moving hazard's sweep (N2; open question 259). |
| Ramp bar (Areas 2 and 3) | The tilapia chase; a longest quiet stretch of at most 480 pixels; no moving hazard type in Area 2 and at most one in Area 3. No numeric short leash is checked (N2; open question 259). |
| Tadpole limits | Speed above 0 and at most 72 pixels per second, 0.8 x a one-leg axolotl's 90. Lane length 80 to 160 pixels; 120 pixels in Areas 2 to 5. Lane offsets from 0 up to 2 x length / speed seconds; lane n of N gets (n − 1) x (2 x length / speed) / N. Wiggle: 4 tail frames at 8 frames a second and a body sway of up to 8 degrees each way (H4; open question 271). |
| Tadpole speed by area (phase 2 starting values) | Area 2: 25 pixels per second. Area 3: 35. Area 4: 48. Area 5: 60. Area 6 on: 60 to 72, never slower than the area before (H4; open question 271). |
| Freshwater shrimp limits | Trigger radius above the 36 pixel eat reach. Rest at least 1.0 second. Flick distance 24 to 72 pixels. Flick speed above 0 and at most 150 pixels per second. Catch limit: rest x 70 ≥ trigger radius + flick distance − 36 (H4; open question 272). |
| Freshwater shrimp by area (phase 2 starting values) | Trigger radius 60 pixels in every area. Rest, flick distance, and flick speed: Area 2, 2.0 seconds, 36 pixels, 100 pixels per second; Area 3, 1.7, 44, 115; Area 4, 1.4, 52, 130; Area 5, 1.2, 60, 145; Area 6 on, Area 5's or harder, inside the catch limit, with a flick speed of 145 to 150 (H4; open question 272). |
| Mobile snack counts by area (phase 2 starting values) | Tadpoles + shrimp, of the same total: Area 2, 2 + 2 of 20; Area 3, 4 + 4 of 24; Area 4, 7 + 7 of 28; Area 5, 10 + 9 of 32; the worms and bugs lowered by the same number. Snacks in the current stay 4, all worms or bugs (H4; open question 270). |
| Mobile snack meet ranges (fact cards) | Tadpole: 120 pixels. Freshwater shrimp: 120 pixels. Each measured from an uncollected snack where it is drawn, outside the safe area (C13; open question 276). |
| Shell perk (Epic and Legendary only) | Blocks one hit. Cap: 1 hit per trip in total. |

### Conflicts with existing docs

| Existing doc says | Conflict | Resolution |
|---|---|---|
| [ADR 0007](../adr/0007-kid-safety-and-privacy.md): "No harsh fail states." | Losing legs and being carried home is a fail state. | Amended by [ADR 0008](../adr/0008-gentle-fail-state.md). |
| An "invulnerability flash" after a hit. | The [art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) limits flashing. | Use a steady, non-flashing protected cue (A5). |
| [Art style guide](../art-style-guide.md): accessories "never cover the eyes". | Glasses are a planned Wearable. | Resolved in the [art style guide](../art-style-guide.md): glasses keep the eyes visible. The Face slot has eye zones, and Face items draw below the eyes (6a, 6i). |
| [Art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) (section 7b) has no rarity colours, badge shapes, Home Tank kit, collectibles, or hazard art. | The MVP needs all of these. | Resolved in the [art style guide](../art-style-guide.md): rarity colours (3e), badge shapes (8), the tilapia (7c), the Home Spot (7d), treasures, the Dash pot, the hint sparkle, and Paint shells (7e), and the Home Tank kit (7f). Section 7b is updated. |
| [Art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) has no Wearable slot anchors, no face, gill, or spot variants, and no Dream colour patterns. | The character creator, outfits, and Dream colours need all of these. | Resolved in the [art style guide](../art-style-guide.md): Wearable slot anchors for all eight slots (6i, issue #16), face, gill, and spot variants (6l to 6n), and Dream colour patterns (6p). |
| AGENTS.md: "no network requests". [ADR 0007](../adr/0007-kid-safety-and-privacy.md): "no third-party requests". | An update check may make a request. | First-party only (E5), plus an ADR (open question 8). |
| [Art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) (6i) has four Wearable slots and layers 8.5 to 11.5. Its Wearable test pose (6k) checks four slots. | F8 now has eight slots, layering, and the feet rule. | Resolved (issue #16). The [art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) now has all eight slots: anchors, footprints, layers, and keep-clear zones for Shirt, Back, Socks, and Feet (6a, 6i), the eight-slot Wearable test pose (6k), and how socks and shoes hide on a lost leg and come back once it has fully regrown (6i, 6q). |
| [ADR 0008](../adr/0008-gentle-fail-state.md) says only unbanked trip finds are lost when carried home. | A12 also starts the snacks over. | Snacks are not banked progress, so ADR 0008's guarantee holds. Amended by [ADR 0010](../adr/0010-carried-home-full-reset.md): being carried home is a full trip reset, snacks included (issue #31). |
| [Art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) (7e) says the hint star is the cue for a hidden treasure. | H3 also uses it on the last 3 snacks. | Resolved in the [art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) (issue #31): the last 3 snacks each show the Common hint star, always on (7e, Last-snack hint star; open question 16). Follow-up: rename "hint star" to "hint sparkle" across the art style guide, which keeps "hint star" for now. |

### Feature priority order

1. A: predator, legs, trip finds, banking, and the Home Spot.
2. B: hidden treasures and rarity, including Paint shells.
3. C: rescue, the Home Tank, Decorations and Wearables, the Collection Book, and morphs.
4. F: the character creator and outfits: slots, perks, choosing the explorer, and Dream colours.
5. D: save and load.
6. E: updates.
7. H: Area 1 exit, snacks to open it, and stars.

Feature A needs something to bank and somewhere to go home before B and C exist.
Early slices may use a placeholder find and an interim home.

### Glossary

| Term | Definition | Avoid |
|---|---|---|
| Trip | One outing from the Home Tank into an area and back. | run, level, life |
| Home Spot | The safe, clearly marked place where each trip starts and where finds are banked. | checkpoint, base |
| Home Tank | The hub where owned axolotls live and decorations are placed. | fish tank (axolotls are amphibians) |
| Trip Bag | Holds unbanked finds for the current trip only. | inventory |
| Trip find | A treasure or rescued axolotl collected this trip but not yet banked. | |
| Banking / Banked | Moving trip finds to permanent ownership at the Home Spot. | saving ("save" means device storage) |
| Treasure Chest | The list of every treasure, in Decorations, Wearables, and Dream colours sections: owned ones in full, unfound ones as silhouettes with "???", their rarity, and a hint naming their area and where to look. Each section has a line saying what its items are for (C11). | inventory |
| Treasure | A hidden collectible: a Decoration, a Wearable, or a Paint shell. | loot |
| Decoration | A treasure placed in the Home Tank. | loot |
| Placement kind | Where a Decoration goes in the Home Tank, set in the catalogue: Floor (the default; it stands on the gravel), Floating (it rests on the water surface), or Hanging (it hangs from the glass top). Floating and Hanging Decorations move left and right only (C9). | slot (a Slot is where a Wearable goes) |
| Wearable | A treasure an axolotl can wear. | loot |
| Rarity | How rare a treasure is: Common, Rare, Epic, or Legendary. | |
| Stranded axolotl | A lost axolotl in an area, such as the canal or the pond, waiting to be rescued. | |
| Rescue | Leading a stranded axolotl home and banking it. | |
| Morph | An axolotl colour type, such as Leucistic or Wild. | |
| Collection Book | The in-game book of morphs and fact cards. In-game nickname: Axolotl-dex. | |
| Fun-fact card | A short, true fact about axolotls or their world. | |
| First-find card | A once-per-save card in the Home Tank, after the tank arrival card, that says what the first banked Decoration, Wearable, or Paint shell is for (C15). | |
| Tilapia | The invasive fish that patrol the canal, the pond, and the chinampa gardens. The first predator. From Area 4 on, each area plays them one step up the predator ladder (N2). | monster |
| Heron | A wading bird that hunts near the surface in Area 3, the lily pad pond's signature predator. Two herons stand in the water and wade slowly along short stretches. A heron hunts by sight: it warns by freezing with its neck drawn into an S, then stabs fast at the spot where the axolotl was. It cannot see an axolotl in cover (reeds, under a lily pad, or the root arch), and the water below its reach is safe. It never bumps, and it is never shown catching the axolotl (N3). | monster |
| Garter snake | A striped snake that hunts at the surface in Area 5, the chinampa gardens' signature predator. Two snakes swim slowly along short stretches at the surface in a gentle S-wiggle. A snake warns by distance, stopping while ripple rings spread from its head, then darts. Cover does not hide the axolotl from it, but the water below its reach is safe ("stay deep"). Touching one gives a gentle bump, and it is never shown biting or catching the axolotl (N3). | monster |
| Common carp | A big, round, bronze-gold invasive fish that roots along the bed and sweeps its tail in a slow, wide strike, warning first by tipping its nose down and puffing mud. A danger part, built in issue #117 and in no shipped area yet. Touching one gives a gentle bump, and it is never shown biting or catching the axolotl (N3). | monster |
| Predator | A kind of hazard: an animal that patrols, warns, and then strikes, like the tilapia. All predators share one set of rules, each with its own species data, and only one warns or strikes at a time (N3). | monster |
| Chase rule | The shared, opt-in rule by which a predator on patrol leaves its route to follow an exposed axolotl in sight, on its leash, no nearer than its lunge range, then returns to its route. Chasing is not attacking: any number of predators may chase at once (N3). | attack (a chase is not a strike) |
| Leash | How far, in pixels, a chasing predator may go from its patrol loop. A garter snake's leash counts as 0, so it chases along its stretch only (N3). | |
| Telegraph | The warning before a lunge: the tilapia's glint and wind-up pose, the heron's S-neck pose, or the garter snake's ripple rings. | |
| Lunge | A predator's strike: the tilapia's quick dash at the axolotl, the heron's stab at the spot fixed when its warning started, or the garter snake's dart. | |
| Hit | A predator's strike that connects, such as a tilapia's lunge, or touching a hazard that costs a leg. It costs one leg unless a Shell perk blocks it (A22, N3). | |
| Protected time | A short time after a hit when no second hit can land. | invulnerability (in UI) |
| Leg loss | Losing one leg to a hit. | injury, wound, damage |
| Regrowth | A lost leg growing back. | healing |
| Carried home | Ending a trip after losing the fourth leg. | death, die, game over, wipe, fail |
| Snack | A worm, bug, tadpole, or freshwater shrimp the axolotl eats by touching it with its head. Not a trip find. Speeds up regrowth while a leg is missing. Every snack counts the same, and every snack must be eaten to open the exit. A plastic worm is not a snack. | |
| Mobile snack | A snack that moves on its own: a tadpole or a freshwater shrimp. Fixed area data, never seeded. Built in issue #117 and in no shipped area yet (H4). | |
| Tadpole | A mobile snack that wiggles there and back along a short lane at its area's speed, never faster than a one-leg axolotl swims (H4). | |
| Freshwater shrimp | A mobile snack that rests, then flicks backward away from the head when it comes near, again after each rest. A repeat darter: in an area with shrimp, they replace the one-time darters (H4). | |
| Lane | A tadpole's short, straight there-and-back route, 80 to 160 pixels long (H4). | route (a route is a predator's) |
| Snack ramp | The rule, in H4, for how an area's share of mobile snacks and their speeds grow area by area: none in Area 1, rising to a cap of about 70%. Every area issue from Area 2 on must meet it (N2). | |
| Save | Progress kept in device storage, by itself: banked treasures, the axolotls, the tank, the Collection Book, and stars. Never anything from a trip in progress (D2). | banking (banking moves trip finds to ownership) |
| Start over | Erasing all saved progress on this device after two confirmations, from the title screen (D7). | |
| Area | A named part of the world, such as the canal (Area 1). Each area is a level: reaching its open exit beats it. Each area is in one habitat, and is built from its area data (H1). | map; "level" in UI text (say "Area 1") |
| Habitat | A group of one or more areas with its own look, such as the canal habitat, which holds Area 1, the lily pad pond, which holds Areas 2 and 3, and the chinampa gardens, which hold Areas 4 and 5 (N2, X9). An area may use its own variant of its habitat's look, such as Area 3's dusk look, `pond-dusk`; it is still in that habitat. | "area" for a habitat |
| Exit | The way out of an area. It stays closed until every snack in the area is eaten. Reaching the open exit beats the area. | |
| Star | One of up to 3 rewards for beating an area. A star, once earned, is kept forever. | |
| Special treasure | The one treasure per area that earns a star when the area is beaten with it owned. Always Common or Rare. | |
| Hazard | Something in an area that can slow the axolotl or cost a leg. A predator, such as the tilapia, is one kind. Each other hazard either costs a leg when touched or only slows (N3). The plastic worm, in Area 2, is the first other hazard to ship, and the ghost net, in Area 4, the second; both only slow, and more come in later areas (N2). A moving hazard costs a leg when touched. | |
| Moving hazard | A hazard that moves on a fixed cycle, warns first, and costs one leg when touched, such as the punting pole. It is not a predator, so it is outside the one-at-a-time rule. A danger part, built in issue #117 and in no shipped area yet (N3). | |
| Punting pole | The first moving hazard: a trajinera's pole that jabs straight down from the surface on a 4 second cycle, after its boat's shadow darkens and its tip dips with a ripple ring (N3). | |
| Danger bar | The full bar, in N2, that every area from Area 4 on must meet: about one moving threat per screen, at least 2 predator species and 1 moving hazard type, every predator chasing, and no quiet stretch of the main path longer than one screen. | |
| Ramp bar | The gentler bar, in N2, for Areas 2 and 3: the tilapia chase on a short leash, no quiet stretch longer than one screen, and at most one moving hazard type (none in Area 2). | |
| Danger part | A predator species marked as one, such as the common carp, or any moving hazard type. Danger parts are outside the one-new-thing rule (N2). | |
| Plastic worm | A hazard in the lily pad pond: a stiff, shiny bit of plastic shaped like a worm. Eating one makes the axolotl queasy. It only slows: it is never a hit, never a snack, and not a threat (N3). | "worm" alone (a worm is a snack) |
| Queasy | How the explorer feels for 3 seconds after eating a plastic worm: a lower top speed, green cheeks, and a wobble. No leg is lost, and it never stacks (N3). | sick, poisoned, damage |
| Ghost net | A hazard in the chinampa gardens: a torn, loose, old fishing net anchored in place. Swimming into one snags the axolotl. It only slows: it is never a hit and not a threat (N3). | fishing line (lines are Area 10's lures), trap |
| Drifting ghost net | A ghost net that drifts slowly back and forth along a short, fixed path, with the current. It snags where it is now and never costs a leg. Built in issue #117 and in no shipped area yet (N3). | |
| Water hyacinth mat | One of a pair of floating plant mats in Area 5, the chinampa gardens' timing element. On an 8 second cycle the two mats of a pair drift together to close the narrow channel between them, then drift apart to open it. A closed channel blocks the explorer, who waits or goes round. A mat is not a hazard: it never costs a leg, is never a hit, and never traps the axolotl, which it pushes out along the channel (N3). | trap; "hazard" (a mat is not one) |
| Snagged | How the explorer is for 2 seconds after swimming into a ghost net: a lower top speed while it wriggles free, with a small snag cue. No leg is lost, and it never stacks (N3). | trapped, caught (the axolotl never looks trapped, ADR 0007) |
| Refuge | The safe axolotl refuge in Xochimilco, opened from the Home Tank, that grows as areas are beaten: one section per habitat, worked out from the saved stars (N2). | |
| Refuge section | One habitat's part of the refuge: hidden until one of its areas is beaten, clean once all of them are, else dirty, with one trash batch per area (N2). | |
| Trash batch | One area's trash in its habitat's refuge section, cleared once the area is beaten (N2). | |
| Habitat milestone | A trip that newly makes a refuge section clean. It shows the "All clean!" card back in the Home Tank. Every habitat has one, and none is the end (N2). | finale, ending, "refuge complete" |
| Refuge nudge | The "Refuge grew" badge on the Refuge tile while a clean-up is waiting to show (N2). | |
| Area map | The screen after Go! on the explorer card where the player picks an unlocked area to explore or replay, on a winding path grouped by habitat (N2). Built in issue #73. | |
| Tank Top Model | Trying on Wearables and posing in a spotlight, started from the Home Tank (N1). Not in the MVP. | |
| Wearable set | A themed group of Wearables that unlocks a small bonus when worn together (N5). A Wearable's optional set ID says which set it belongs to. | |
| Character creator | The step-by-step screens at first launch where the player makes a starter axolotl: 11 steps, one choice per step, from Colour to Name (F1). | |
| Boy or girl | Whether an axolotl is a boy or a girl. Exactly one, always set, and fixed after creation. Text about one axolotl says "he" or "she". | gender; "sex" in UI text; "they" or "it" for one axolotl |
| Tail (look option) | How long an axolotl's tail is: Short, Medium, or Long. Looks only. Not the Tail Wearable slot, and not Tail Dash. | |
| Body | An axolotl's body shape: Slim, Regular, or Chubby. Looks only. | |
| Age | How grown-up an axolotl looks: Baby, Young, or Grown-up. Changes proportions only, never size. | |
| Size | How big an axolotl is drawn: Small, Medium, or Large. Looks only: every size has the same hit area and speed. | |
| Picture button | A big button on a creator or restyle step that shows one option as a small preview on the axolotl so far. | |
| Name box | The field on the Name step where the player types the axolotl's name. It looks typeable: a light inset field, a pencil, the "Type a name" placeholder, and a blinking cursor while focused (F17). | |
| Restyle | Changing an owned axolotl's look and name in the Home Tank, on the creator's step screens with tabs, putting on or taking off its Dream colour on its own tab, and choosing its outfit on the Outfit tab. Colour and boy or girl cannot change. | |
| Outfit | The Wearables one axolotl has on, one per slot at most. | |
| Slot | One of eight places a Wearable goes: Head, Neck, Face, Shirt, Back, Tail, Socks, or Feet. | |
| Perk | The one small gameplay bonus a Wearable gives on trips. | stat, buff (in UI) |
| Perk cap | The hard limit on the total of one perk type in an outfit. | |
| Explorer | The axolotl the player takes on a trip, chosen on the explorer card before each trip. Only its outfit applies. | |
| Real morph | One of the eight true axolotl morphs: Leucistic, Wild, Golden albino, White albino, Copper, Piebald, Axanthic, or Melanoid. | |
| Dream colour | A fantasy colour, solid or patterned, that can be put on any owned axolotl. Patterned and sheen ones are Epic or Legendary; solid ones are Common or Rare. Not found in nature. | skin |
| Paint shell | A hidden treasure that holds one Dream colour. | |
| Hiding spot | A place in an area where a treasure can hide. Each trip picks one per treasure picked for that trip. | |
| Treasure slot | One of an area's set number of places for a treasure on each trip. The trip seed picks each slot's rarity by chance (B4). Not a Wearable slot. | |
| Dash pot | A pot only Tail Dash can break. Legendary treasures hide in one. | |
